Request: `{entry: str}` (the operator's typed input — could be dotted Java form, partial Smali, or full stack-trace line). Response: `{normalised_entry: str | null, class_exists_in_graph: bool, method_count: int, error: str | null}`. Logic: (1) parse the input with a small heuristic dispatcher (dotted form `com.example.Foo.onClick` → Smali prefix `Lcom/example/Foo;->onClick(`; partial Smali stays as-is; stack-trace line `com.example.Foo.onClick(Foo.java:42)` → Smali prefix); (2) validate against the call graph (`class_exists_in_graph` via `call_graph.list_graph(package_prefix=class_smali)`; `method_count` via the existing `_normalise_smali_class` + method-count count from 10.8's MethodPicker plumbing); (3) return the normalised entry + validation flags. 404 on unknown app; 409 when call graph isn't ready; 422 on un-parseable input (with a clear `error` message the frontend renders inline). **Frontend.** `androscan/web/frontend/src/api/trace.ts` gains a `normaliseTraceEntry(appId, entry)` client function + `NormaliseEntryResponse` type. `LabTraceMode.tsx` adds a debounced effect (400ms) on entry-field change that calls `normaliseTraceEntry`, displays an inline spinner during the call, and renders a ✓ / ⚠ pill with the validation result (✓ "valid class with N methods" / ⚠ "class not found in call graph"). The pill is the entry point for v2.1.3's Tier 1 "Find similar classes" button. New CSS in `App.css` under `.trace-entry-spinner` + `.trace-entry-validation-pill`. **Tests.** `tests/test_trace_routes.py` (+~5-7 — happy path with dotted input, happy path with partial Smali input, stack-trace-line normalisation, class-not-found 200 with `class_exists_in_graph: false`, un-parseable input 422, unknown-app 404, call-graph-not-ready 409). Frontend verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean. **Q5 (A):** translate class+method only (no descriptor / overload resolution — `MethodPicker` from 10.8 already handles overloads). **Q6 (B):** backend coalescer (not frontend-only) so we get authoritative call-graph validation in the same round-trip — frontend-only translation would lack the validation signal, and validation is what makes the spinner / pill meaningful. | v2.1.1 |

Backend logic: (1) fast path — fuzzy-match the input class name against the call graph's class list via `difflib.get_close_matches` (no LLM; sub-100ms; covers typos like `MainActvity` → `MainActivity`); (2) if fuzzy match yields no results AND the input has at least 3 word-segments, fall back to the new `suggest_trace_entry` skill from v2.1.5 (LLM-backed semantic search; ~500ms-2s). | v2.1.11 | **[x] Post-release patch — Frida-server Settings card surfaces "running but unverified-root" amber state + per-mode Diagnose playbook** *(landed 2026-05-20 — frontend-only; closes an operator-visible UX bug surfaced during dynamic-trace dogfood on the LCP-track infra — `frida-server` reported green / "running" in the Status card while `POST /api/frida/attach` returned 503 with "unable to connect to remote frida-server: closed")* | Single atomic ship vehicle (single-bug-with-architectural-seam posture). **Root cause:** the backend probe's three-layer detection strategy in `androscan/web/health_probes.probe_frida_server` returns `running=True` when ANY of Layer 1 (`pidof frida-server`), Layer 2 (`ps -A | grep frida-server*`), or Layer 3 (`frida-ps -U`) hits — but only Layers 1 + 2 set the `uid` field (read from `/proc/<pid>/status`). A Layer-3-only hit (renamed binary, frida-gadget, or non-root server whose comm doesn't match the L1/L2 prefix filter) returns `running=True && uid=None`. Pre-v2.1.11 `FridaServerStatusCard` painted the dot green whenever `card.ok === true`, and the probe's `ok` mirrored `running` — so the operator-visible card looked identical between "verified up as root" and "reachable but root unverified". The `uidWarning` extras line was gated on `card.uid != null && card.uid !== "root"`, silently skipping the `uid == null` case. The Start-as-root button's visibility (`needsStart`) had the same gate. **What v2.1.11 ships.** Three changes in `androscan/web/frontend/src/tabs/SettingsTab.tsx` (single file, ~120 LOC net add): (1) **`StatusCardView` gains `overrideDot?: "ok" | "warn" | "fail"`** that lets callers paint the dot color when they know more about the card's health than `card.ok` conveys; all other callers keep their existing behaviour (omitted prop falls back to the existing `card.ok / skipped / card.error` ladder). (2) **`FridaServerStatusCard`** collapses both amber sub-cases into a single `unverifiedRoot` signal (`card.running && (card.uid == null || card.uid !== "root")`) that drives the amber tint (`overrideDot={unverifiedRoot ? "warn" : undefined}`) + the extended `needsStart = !card.running || unverifiedRoot`. Two distinct `uidWarning` lines for the two sub-cases (known non-root says "running as `${uid}` — app attaches will fail unless restarted as root"; unverified says "uid: unknown — host-side enumeration only; root cannot be verified, app attaches may fail"). Pre-v2.1.11 host-confirmed copy rewritten to operator-readable form that ties the label to the amber state. (3) **New `FridaServerDiagnoseHint` component** (~60 LOC; reuses `.frida-install-*` CSS for visual parity with the red-state Install playbook + zero new CSS): mode `"known-non-root"` surfaces a single command (`adb shell "pgrep -f frida-server \| xargs -r kill -9"` — matches `frida_routes.start_frida_server`'s 409-detail text so operator can preview the kill before clicking Start); mode `"unverified"` surfaces a two-step playbook (`adb shell "ps -A \| grep -iE 'frida\|gadget'"` to find the PID + binary name → `adb shell "su 0 kill -9 <PID>"` with `<PID>` as a plain-text placeholder, not auto-substituted). Both playbooks omit the "start as root" command — the Start button below is the single source of truth (sourcing from `_FRIDA_SERVER_BIN` so no drift risk). **Status type doc-block updates** in `androscan/web/frontend/src/api/status.ts` `frida_server.uid` field documenting the v2.1.11 `null`-as-unverified-presumed-non-root treatment + the per-mode playbook + the extended button visibility. **No backend changes** — `health_probes.py` + `frida_routes.py` + `frida_client.py` unchanged. The backend probe's permissive-by-design `ok` posture (liveness, not feature-readiness) stays as-is; the "ok-but-broken-for-attaches" nuance is a UX-side concern that lives at the rendering boundary where the operator sees it. **Verification gates:** `pytest` 1290/1290 (no test changes; bug was UI-side rendering / signal-derivation only; the +166 from v2.1.10's 1124 baseline come from Phase 13 sub-step ships 13.0 → 13.10 + v2.0 + v2.0.1, all unrelated to the v2.1 follow-up release umbrella); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean (1511.76 KB JS / 472.85 KB gzip / 131.18 KB CSS / 21.03 KB gzip — absolute size is now Phase-13-v2.x-dominated post the React Flow / `@xyflow/react` adoption); `ReadLints` clean across both modified files (`SettingsTab.tsx` + `status.ts`). **Operator impact:** (1) Settings card visually distinguishes verified-root (green) from unverified / non-root (amber). (2) Start button now visible for both amber sub-cases (was hidden for unverified pre-v2.1.11). (3) Diagnose playbook previewable inline — kill commands no longer buried in the post-click 409 error div, click-fail-copy-from-error-text-click loop collapses to copy-paste-click. **Architectural lesson:** green probe ≠ green UX — a permissive backend probe (liveness) + a strict UI rendering boundary (feature-readiness) lets each layer stay simple at its own scope; `StatusCardView.overrideDot` is the cross-cutting seam for that pattern across the Settings tab. | v2.1.10 |
| v2.1.10 | **[x] Post-release patch — three-bug operator-dogfood bundle: Lab-mode state preservation across hops + Trace JSON column repair in narrow panes + Settings scroller migration to page-right edge** *(landed 2026-05-07 — frontend-only; closes three independent operator-reported bugs surfaced in a single dogfood session on the v2.1.9 ship vehicle)* | Single atomic ship vehicle (the three bugs were small enough to land in one ship; they share the v2.1 follow-up release umbrella and post-release-patch posture). **Bug 1 — Lab-mode state lost on hop-and-return.** Pre-v2.1.10 `LabTab.tsx` conditionally rendered Trace / Manual Hooks / Graph keyed on `labMode === <id>` (full unmount of inactive modes); operators lost their entire Trace state (entry draft, loaded `BehaviorAnchor`, decompile tree, similar-classes candidates, picker results) every time they hopped to Manual Hooks and back. **Fix:** Trace + Manual Hooks always-mounted via a new `.lab-mode-content-pane` wrapper that uses `display: contents` (active) and `[hidden]` UA-`display: none` (inactive); children act as direct flex items of `.lab-mode-content` exactly as pre-v2.1.10 (mirrors the existing `.lab-tab-host[hidden]` / `.settings-tab-host[hidden]` precedent). Graph mode stays conditional per operator scope choice on the v2.1.10 design questionnaire (Cytoscape Web Worker is measurably expensive to keep idle; viewport state already cached by the cytoscape instance). The 11.3 doc-block at LabTab.tsx lines 436-441 (which described the freshness-via-remount pattern Manual Hooks's `anchoredMethods` overlay used to rely on) is replaced by an explicit `traceCacheVersion: number` + `bumpTraceCacheVersion()` counter on `WorkbenchContext` (mirrors the local `cachedReloadTick` / `sessionsRefreshTick` patterns inside LabTab.tsx but lifted to context because Trace and Manual Hooks are now siblings rather than mutually-exclusive conditional renders). `LabTraceMode` calls `bumpTraceCacheVersion()` from the post-Build effect (`state.kind === "loaded" && state.from === "build"`) and from `onDeleteCached` on `r.ok`; `ManualHooksMode`'s `anchoredMethods` effect adds the counter to its dep array. Counter is monotonic across `appId` changes — the consumer's effect already keys on `appId` so a stale counter from a previous app is harmless; keeping it monotonic simplifies reasoning ("counter went up → re-fetch"). **Bug 2 — Trace JSON column rendered as a tall narrow column instead of a row in narrow Lab panes.** The `.trace-row-meta` CSS Grid (`grid-template-columns: 1.2rem 6.5rem 3.4rem 4rem auto 1fr`) had no compressibility constraints on the `auto` label track — an unbreakable label like `onCreate$lambda$0_trace` (no spaces / hyphens / break opportunities) forced the track to its full max-content width (~17 rem at 0.74 rem font), leaving the `1fr` payload track at near-zero in narrow panes (Manual Hooks right column defaults to 22% of Lab tab width = ~19 rem total). Expanded rows then wrapped JSON every ~8-10 chars via `overflow-wrap: anywhere`. **Fix:** added `min-width: 0; max-width: 14rem; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap;` to `.trace-label` so the label compresses with `…` truncation when the row is narrow, freeing the payload column to take the remaining width; full label still readable via the row's existing `title={compact}` tooltip + the chevron-expand renders the label inline in the payload's first key. 14rem cap chosen to comfortably fit a typical Smali method name without truncation while still giving the payload column the headroom it needs for JSON to wrap onto wide lines. **Bug 3 — Settings sub-tab vertical scrollbar offset from the page right edge.** The Settings panel chain (`.settings-tab` grid 240px | 1fr → `.settings-panel-host` overflow:hidden → `.settings-panel` max-width:1100px margin:0 auto padding:16px 24px 0 → `.settings-panel-body` overflow:auto) put the scroll on the inner `.settings-panel-body`, so the scrollbar sat at the inner panel's right edge — typically 24px (panel right padding) inside the right column on a narrow viewport, more on wider ones (centered panel adds 30+ px margins). Operator screenshot showed a ~50px gap between the scrollbar and the browser window's right edge. **Fix:** promoted the scroller from `.settings-panel-body` up to `.settings-panel-host` (`overflow-y: auto; overflow-x: hidden;`); reduced `.settings-panel-body` to a plain content block (dropped `flex: 1` + `min-height: 0` + `overflow: auto`); made `.settings-panel-header` `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 2; background: var(--bg);` so it pins to the top of the host's scroll viewport (same operator UX as before — header always visible while body scrolls — but the scrollbar now lives on the host element which is the right edge of the `1fr` column inside `.settings-tab`, i.e. the absolute right of the page minus the 240px nav). Header's `margin-bottom: 16px` folded into `.settings-panel-body { padding-top: 16px }` so the visual spacing stays the same (the margin would otherwise leave a transparent strip below the sticky header where scrolled content could show through). Affects all four Settings sub-panels (Global / App / Status / Diagnostics) since they share the `.settings-panel` shell — bonus consistency win. **Verification gates:** `pytest` 1124/1124 (no test changes from v2.1.10 — all three bugs are UI-mounting-shape / CSS only; the +31 from v2.1.9's 1093 baseline come from two interim commits — `526016b` ollama-think CLI flag + `ee54902` consolidation fix — that landed between v2.1.9 and v2.1.10 on the same calendar day, both unrelated to the v2.1 follow-up release umbrella); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean (1238.79 KB JS / 385.33 KB gzip / 94.54 KB CSS / 15.89 KB gzip — JS +0.28 KB raw / +0.10 KB gzip vs v2.1.9 baseline; CSS +0.21 KB raw / +0.04 KB gzip; well within the LCP.0 ceiling of +5 KB JS / +2 KB CSS); `ReadLints` clean across all 4 production-code files. | v2.1.9 |

**Risks (per v2.1.0 ratification):**
- **Coalescer false-negatives misleading operators on legitimate-but-not-yet-built classes:** the call-graph-validation step in v2.1.2 returns `class_exists_in_graph: false` for any class not in the call graph SQLite — including classes that exist in the decompiled source but haven't been picked up by the call-graph builder (extremely rare, but possible if the call-graph build had errors that silently dropped the class). Mitigation: the validation pill copy is "class not found in call graph" rather than "class doesn't exist" so operators understand the scope of the check; v2.1.3's "Find similar classes" button also covers this case (the LLM fallback will surface the legitimate class as a candidate even if the fuzzy-match misses it).
- **LLM budget squeeze on v2.1.5's `suggest_trace_entry` skill:** the skill combines RAG hits + call-graph context + an LLM ranking pass — the per-skill output budget of ~6 KB (DEC-022) might get tight on apps with very long class names + verbose rationale. Mitigation: the skill caps each rationale at 200 chars + the candidates list at 3; if the budget squeeze surfaces in operator use, tighten the per-decision prose payload (same playbook DEC-025 used for the v2 LLM-budget bumps).
- **Chat-widget pattern adoption beyond v2.1.5:** Q7 (ii) is deliberately scoped to "ship the pattern with one consumer in v2.1, add more in subsequent releases as demand surfaces." The risk is the pattern becomes load-bearing across multiple skills and the schema needs a breaking change before v2.2 lands. Mitigation: the `SkillWidget` typed union is additive-by-design (new widget kinds add as new union members without breaking the schema); the chat renderer's `<ChatWidgetRenderer>` dispatcher gracefully handles unknown widget kinds (renders the skill's `text` field only, ignores the unknown widget). DEC-022 cross-link in DEC-025's v2.1 closing-note documents this discipline so future contributors don't trip over the schema-additivity rule.
- **`pendingChatPrefill` plumbing collision with future cross-tab handoff patterns:** v2.1.4 introduces a fourth `pending*Prefill` plumbing seam in `WorkbenchContext` (alongside `pendingTraceEntry` / `pendingHookPrefill` / `pendingCodeNav`). If the count grows beyond 5-6, refactor into a single `pendingHandoff: {kind, payload}` discriminated union — but that's a v3 concern, not v2.1's. Mitigation: keep the 4-seam pattern for v2.1; revisit if v3 / Phase 12 scope adds a fifth seam.
- **Sub-step ordering risk:** v2.1.3 (Tier 1) depends on v2.1.5's `suggest_trace_entry` skill if it wants the LLM-backed fallback; if the spec says v2.1.3 ships before v2.1.5, the Tier 1 sub-step will ship with fuzzy-match-only and v2.1.5 will wire in the LLM fallback as part of its own scope. Mitigation: the sub-step backlog table's "Depends on" column flags this explicitly so the implementing agent knows which order to ship.

**§ Phase 13 — Behavior Trace v3 — Dynamic Execution + Flowchart UI overhaul — sub-step backlog** *(strictly linear; one sub-step per Agent-mode session; ratified in 13.0 2026-05-08 — DEC-029; mirrors Phase 11's 11.0 → 11.7 cadence)*

Phase 13 ships three coherent deliverables on top of the unchanged Phase 11 v2 / v2.1 static substrate: (1) **UI overhaul** — rename "Decision Timeline" → "Behavior Trace"; replace the linear timeline with a flowchart + Inspector pane; remove predicate-register / instruction-index / `true NEUTRAL` / `false NEUTRAL` noise from the primary UI (can resurface behind an "Advanced" toggle in a future sub-step if asked). (2) **Dynamic execution trace** via a new `behavior_trace_multi` Frida template that hooks every method in the active `BehaviorAnchor`'s closure, captures entry/exit events with a per-thread depth counter, stringifies arguments and return values via a tiered helper, streams events over the existing `/ws/trace` WebSocket, and persists to `apps/<app_id>/<run>/dynamic_trace.jsonl`. (3) **Per-method LLM summaries** via a new `summarise_method` skill (`requires_confirmation=False`); live-on-first-hit for methods that fire dynamically OR are explicitly clicked in Inspector; cached in `skill_results_cache` keyed on `(app_sha, class_smali, method_name, descriptor)`; multiplexed on the trace WebSocket as `summary_pending` / `summary_ready` / `summary_failed` events. **Schema-additive — `BehaviorAnchor` / `DecisionPoint` / `BypassPlan` / `PredicateOrigin` schemas unchanged** (no schema bump on `trace.sqlite`; SCHEMA_VERSION stays at `"2"` from Phase 11 v2). **Locked design details** (per DEC-029, three-round plan-mode iteration on 2026-05-07 → 2026-05-08): three modes (`Static` default before any dynamic run / `Dynamic` / `Both` overlay default after first dynamic run); edge styling = color-only emphasis (same 1.5px stroke + 6×6 arrowhead for all edges; fired = accent blue + full opacity + solid; untaken = dim/dashed at 55% opacity; the operator explicitly rejected thicker fired edges and a larger arrowhead — "Blue arrow looks fine, but don't make it thick"); run-trace button color thresholds (≤20 hooks green / 21-50 yellow / 51-100 orange / >100 red). **Canonical mockup** at `~/.cursor/projects/Users-sujoy-chakravarti-TW-Work-AI-security-Cursor-AI-AndroScan/canvases/phase-13-trace-mockup.canvas.tsx` (canvas SDK; SVG via `computeDAGLayout`); the production frontend in 13.6 → 13.9 must visually match the mockup within the limits of React-on-DOM. **Explicitly NOT in v1 scope** (revisit in 13.x or in a follow-up DEC): marching-ants animation on fired path; branch-outcome inference from dynamic data; speculative pre-summarisation of every static method; separate "Tree of calls" panel (the flowchart IS the tree); mutation hooks (modify return values mid-run). **Phase 12 / DEC-026 stays untouched** — operator direction was explicit ("Ignore phase 12 stuff for now, we'll come to it later after phase 13"); Phase 12's static-precision menu (ISSUE-014, auto-verify, free-form LLM JS, per-app TTL, iOS adapters, Smali patching, LLM-budget real-app measurement) stays deferred-pending-telemetry.

| 13.3  | **[x] WebSocket multiplexing — `WS /ws/trace/{app_id}/{session_id}` + `summary_pending` / `summary_ready` / `summary_failed` events** *(landed 2026-05-08 — BE only; additive event vocabulary; backwards-compatible)* | **Landed (this commit).** **Wire-shape and seam adjustments incorporated from explore-subagent's report on existing `frida_routes.py` patterns + the `skill_results_cache` API:** (1) Endpoint path is `WS /ws/trace/{app_id}/{session_id}` (not the dependency-line-implied `/ws/trace`) — explicit `app_id` segment is required for the cache-key derivation (cache reads need `app_sha` from the per-app `app_meta.json`; bundling that into the URL keeps the WS handler stateless, matches `frida_routes.ws_trace`'s style of carrying identifiers in the path rather than in connect-time JSON). (2) `build_trace_router` factory now returns `(rest_router, ws_router)` — same split-prefix posture as `build_frida_router` since the WS prefix differs from the REST prefix. Updated three call sites: production `androscan/web/app.py` + two existing `tests/test_trace_routes.py` 503-path tests. Backwards-compatible at the **call shape** layer (operator running an old image gets a clean `TypeError` at startup rather than a silently-half-wired router); strictly-additive at the **wire** layer (old WS clients filtering on the existing trace `kind`s ignore `summary_*` silently). (3) Summary callable is a fourth optional factory parameter (`SummaryCallable = Callable[[str, str, str], Awaitable[str]]`) — DI seam for tests and for 13.4's skill-tier swap-in. Production default = `default_summary_callable(config_provider)` (thin inline `androscan.llm.client.complete` call wrapped in `loop.run_in_executor` + `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=30.0)`); v1 prompt is name-quality only (class + method + descriptor; no decompiled source) — 13.4 will enrich with a source snippet via `decompile_cache` + register a formal `summarise_method` skill, but the seam stays so 13.4's swap-in is a one-line change in `app.py`. (4) Per-method dedup via in-memory `Set[str]` keyed on `MethodRef.smali_signature`-shaped strings (`method_key()` helper in `trace_summary.py`) — same method firing 50× in a single session triggers one LLM call, not 50; cross-session dedup happens in the `skill_results_cache` layer. (5) Cache-miss flow emits `summary_pending` before spawning the `asyncio.create_task` so the UI sees the pending state even on fast LLM round-trips; **cache-hit** flow skips `summary_pending` entirely (no flicker for the operator's UI). (6) Three new failure modes locked at the wire layer: `error: "summary_timeout"` (`asyncio.TimeoutError`), `error: "empty_summary"` (LLM returned whitespace-only), and `error: "<exception message>"` (other exceptions). Failed summaries are deliberately NOT cached — failures may be transient. (7) WebSocket lifecycle mirrors `frida_routes.ws_trace` exactly: accept → 1008-close on unknown session → ring-buffer replay → register `on_event` hook → forever-pump bounded `asyncio.Queue(maxsize=2000)` → restore previous hook on disconnect → cancel any in-flight summary tasks (so the executor doesn't keep running an LLM call for a client that's already gone). (8) `app_sha` resolution is best-effort — missing `app_meta.json` → cache layer disabled (logged), summary multiplex still emits live but never warms the cache; this fail-soft posture matches `decompile_cache.get_status` for the same condition. **Backend deliverables.** New `androscan/web/trace_summary.py` (~280 LOC) with: `SUMMARY_SKILL_ID = "summarise_method"` (cache discriminator — chosen ahead of 13.4's skill-registration so 13.4's writes hit byte-equal-keyed cache entries; no cache rebuild required at the cutover), `SUMMARY_TIMEOUT_S = 30.0`, `SUMMARY_PENDING_KIND` / `SUMMARY_READY_KIND` / `SUMMARY_FAILED_KIND` constants, `SummaryCallable` async type alias, `java_class_to_smali` (Java-form ↔ Smali-form, idempotent on already-Smali input), `method_key` (Smali-signature shape), `summary_cache_params` (4-tuple cache key), `lookup_cached_summary` / `store_summary` (treat-empty-as-miss discipline + fail-soft on cache I/O errors), `default_summary_callable` (production wiring). `androscan/web/trace_routes.py` extended with the WS endpoint (~270 LOC inside the factory closure: `_dispatch_summary_for_entry` per-event hook, three `_emit_summary_*` helpers, `_fire_summary` background task with full exception ladder, replay + live-pump branches both routing through `_dispatch`); imports + factory signature + return type updated; module-level `_now_ts` helper added for test seam. `androscan/web/app.py` rewired to unpack the `(rest, ws)` tuple and `include_router` both. **Tests.** `tests/test_trace_summary.py` (NEW; **+21 tests** across `java_class_to_smali` (4 — dotted/Smali/$inner/empty), `method_key` (2 — round-trip + empty-descriptor), `summary_cache_params` (2 — full-shape + class-form normalisation), cache round-trip discipline (5 — miss/hit/empty-as-miss/no-op-on-empty/overload separation/per-app separation), and the async `default_summary_callable` (4 — content/whitespace-strip/timeout/exception propagation), wire-shape constants (3 — kind values + skill id + timeout). `tests/test_trace_routes.py` (**+15 WS tests** covering: registration smoke, replay-then-stream pre-existing events, 1008 unknown-session, 1008 no-`frida_client_provider`, cache-miss → pending+ready, cache-hit → ready-only with `cached: true`, per-method dedup (2 entries → 1 summary), exception → `summary_failed`, timeout → `summary_failed`, empty → `summary_failed`, non-`entry` phases (`ready` / `exit` / `hook_failed` / `error`) DON'T fire summaries, no-summary-callable wiring still streams trace events but skips multiplex, live-event path also fires summaries, successful summary writes back to `skill_results_cache.json` for cross-session reuse, failed summaries are NOT cached. | 13.6  | **[x] Frontend: `ExecutionFlow` component (renders alongside Behavior Trace list during 13.6 → 13.8 build-out)** *(landed 2026-05-08 — FE-only; uses `@xyflow/react` + a pure DAG-extraction helper)* | **Landed (this commit).** **Planning checkpoint resolution.** The substrate question (hand-rolled SVG vs. React Flow vs. hybrid) was resolved as **option (b) — React Flow / `@xyflow/react`** per operator preference. Rationale traded the ~150 KB JS bundle bump for built-in pan / zoom / minimap, drag-pan inertia, and a familiar consumer-side API; the locked DEC-029 styling (1.5px stroke + 6×6 arrowhead + color-only emphasis + 220×72 cards) is enforced via custom node + custom edge components that override the library defaults rather than fighting them. **Frontend deliverables (1 modified, 3 new).** New `androscan/web/frontend/src/components/trace/executionFlowGraph.ts` (~340 LOC) — pure helper (zero React, zero React Flow dependencies) that converts a `BehaviorAnchor` into the `{ nodes, edges }` shape the renderer consumes. Translation rules: one node per `MethodRef` referenced anywhere in the anchor (entry + decision-source + decision-target + plan source + plan target methods), deduped on overload-key (`L<class>;-><method>`) so overloads collapse onto a single stacked-card node carrying `overloadCount`; node `kind` is `entry` / `gate` / `sink_allow` / `sink_deny` / `sink_neutral` / `method` per the DEC-029 corner-pill taxonomy; one edge per `(decision, branch_verdict)` pair routed to either the cross-referenced bypass-plan target method (when `BypassPlan.source_decision_method` + `source_decision_instruction_index` matches the decision) or a synthetic per-source verdict-flavored sink (`__sink_allow__` / `__sink_deny__` / `__sink_neutral__`); coarse layout-rank assigned via BFS from the entry so the renderer can do a column-grid layout without an external lib like dagre. `possiblyInlined` heuristic: method shows up as a decision/plan target but NOT as a decision source → flagged for the dashed-border + "possibly inlined" pill (13.9 will refine this against runtime `hook_failed` events). New `androscan/web/frontend/src/components/trace/ExecutionFlow.tsx` (~330 LOC) — the React Flow renderer. Custom `MethodNode` component renders the locked 220×72 / 6px-radius / three-line stacked-card visual with corner pills (GATE / ENTRY / ALLOW / DENY / NEUTRAL), source-line + overload-count + possibly-inlined meta chips, and dashed border for inlined methods. Custom `VerdictEdge` component renders the locked 1.5px stroke + 6×6 arrowhead via React Flow's `markerEnd: { type: ArrowClosed, width: 6, height: 6 }` + a `getSmoothStepPath` geometry (rounded right-angle bends, matches the mockup); inline edge labels (`allowed` / `denied` / `passes through`) render via `EdgeLabelRenderer` with verdict-flavored chips. `nodesDraggable={false}` and `nodesConnectable={false}` lock the layout; pan / zoom / minimap stay enabled (React Flow's `<Controls>` + `<MiniMap>` re-skinned to the workbench dark palette via the `.execution-flow-*` namespace). `onNodeClick` lifts the selected node to `LabTraceMode.selectedFlowNodeId` (no consumer yet — 13.7's Inspector pane will read it); synthetic sinks don't fire the click handler. `selectedNodeId` is plumbed in as a controlled prop so the consumer drives selection state. `proOptions={{ hideAttribution: true }}` removes the React Flow watermark. `LabTraceMode.tsx` (~+45 LOC) — imports `ExecutionFlow`, adds `executionFlowCollapsed` + `selectedFlowNodeId` state, inserts a new collapsible `<section className="trace-section">` between `BehaviorAnchorCard` and the existing Behavior Trace list section. Component renders five sections inside `<aside className="inspector">`: (1) **Signature** — `<code>` block + `class_name` muted line + `×N overloads` pill when `overloadCount > 1` + `possibly inlined` warn-colored pill + a fuller "R8 may have inlined this; check `hook_failed` events" callout when applicable; (2) **Summary** — empty-state placeholder ("Summary not yet generated. Action row at the bottom (sticky `<footer.inspector-actions>` with 2-col grid): `[Hook this method]` (writes `pendingHookPrefill` with the `entry_exit_log` template — mirrors `BehaviorTrace.onVerify` byte-for-byte, with `event_label = <method>_inspector` for traceability + `sourceLabel = "Inspector: <class>.<method>"` so the HookBuilder header surfaces the origin — and flips `labMode` to `"manual-hooks"`); `[Trace this gate]` (writes `pendingTraceEntry` with `entryPrefix = methodSig(canonical)` — full Smali signature so `useTraceAnchor` auto-fires the cache GET; cache-miss path surfaces the operator-clickable `[Build trace]` button as usual; `setLabMode("trace")` is idempotent in v1 since the Inspector only renders inside Trace mode); `[Open source]` (writes `pendingCodeNav` for the canonical method's class + flips tab to `"inspect"` — same plumbing as `BehaviorAnchorCard.onOpenInInspect`); `[Open source]` spans both grid columns as a secondary full-width row matching the canvas mockup. `[×]` close button at the top of the panel fires `onClear` which the consumer wires to `setSelectedFlowNode(null)`. Synthetic-sink guard renders a "Synthetic sink nodes don't carry a method reference." copy in the unlikely event one slips through. status `Open (Phase 13 v2 candidate; operator-demand-gated)`. **`docs/SAFETY_AND_SECURITY.md` §12.x:** new subsection covering the multi-method tracer's per-app `hook_target_package_prefix` allowlist (already in place from Hook Lab v1) + the threshold-color UX for hook-count visibility + the live-on-first-hit summariser's `requires_confirmation=False` invariant + the JSONL persistence path + a note that dynamic traces are per-run artifacts (not persisted in `trace.sqlite`, so the `SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 → 2 invalidation pattern from Phase 11 v2 doesn't apply to dynamic traces). **Remove `VITE_BEHAVIOR_TRACE_LEGACY` flag** + delete the legacy `DecisionTimeline.tsx` code path (kept around since 13.5 in case operators wanted the old view; one-release migration is enough). Take screenshots of the three modes (Static / Dynamic / Both) on the WeakBank example for the docs. **No code changes** in 13.10 itself except for the legacy-flag removal. | 13.9 |

**Brief Ask-mode planning checkpoint at the top of 13.1** to lock the multi-method tracer JS template shape — three open questions: (a) per-method `Java.use()` failure handling — `try/catch` posture for `inlined?` detection (tag failed methods in the trace event stream so the UI surfaces the `Possibly inlined` callout); (b) depth-counter implementation — per-thread `Map<thread_id, int>` with `Stalker.follow` boundary detection vs. simple per-method `enter` / `leave` increment / decrement; (c) arg/return serialiser tier ordering — `(primitives → java.lang.* common types → user-defined classes via reflection) → fallback toString()`; truncation thresholds (256 chars for strings, 32 bytes for byte arrays, depth 1 / field 8 for object reflection). Mirrors the 10.6 `BehaviorAnchor` JSON wire-shape checkpoint and the 11.4 `_DescentBudget` checkpoint.

**Brief Ask-mode planning checkpoint at the top of 13.6** to pick the `ExecutionFlow` substrate — (a) hand-rolled SVG via a small DAG-layout helper ported from the canvas mockup's `computeDAGLayout`, vs. (b) React Flow with custom nodes/edges. Both are viable; the canvas mockup proves the layout math is small.

**Risks (per DEC-029):**
- **Multi-method Frida tracer's arg/return serialiser is the primary risk surface.** Reflection over user-defined Java classes can hit `IllegalAccessException` on private fields, `NullPointerException` on uninitialised fields, and OOM on circular object graphs. Mitigated by: (a) per-field `try/catch` so one failure doesn't poison the whole event, (b) depth cap (default 1) and field cap (default 8), (c) string truncation at 256 chars, (d) byte-array detection + length-only summary for arrays > 32 bytes, (e) explicit smoke fixtures covering the common Java types in 13.1's tests.
- **Live LLM summaries during a dynamic run can swamp the LLM if many methods fire.** Bounded by `MAX_SKILLS_PER_TURN = 3` from DEC-022 (the agentic loop pattern), per-method timeout (5s default), and the `skill_results_cache` so each method is summarised at most once per app SHA. Worst case: dynamic trace fires 30 unique methods, summariser fires 30 LLM calls 3-at-a-time, with the UI showing `Generating…` for the rest until they arrive.
- **`inlined?` detection has a low but non-zero false-positive rate.** `Java.use()` failing on a method can also be caused by a stale or partial decompile / classpath mismatch. Mitigated by surfacing the result as `Possibly inlined` (not `Inlined`) and pointing operators at the source for verification.
- **Edge styling lock is easy to undo accidentally.** The plan-mode iteration explicitly tested thicker fired edges and a larger arrowhead and the operator rejected both ("Blue arrow looks fine, but don't make it thick"). The lock is documented in DEC-029's `decision:` block + this risks block to keep it sticky — a future contributor should NOT re-thicken the fired path without explicit operator sign-off.
- **Dynamic-trace data is NOT persisted in `trace.sqlite` — it lives at `apps/<app_id>/<run>/dynamic_trace.jsonl`.** Operators expecting cached anchors to also carry dynamic-trace history will need to re-run dynamic traces per session. Mitigated by clear naming (`dynamic_trace.jsonl` vs. cached `BehaviorAnchor`s in `trace.sqlite`); revisit if dogfood shows operators want a cross-session dynamic-trace history.
- **The locked design intentionally drops several power-user diagnostics** (predicate registers, instruction indices, neutral verdict badges). If a power user asks for them back, the right home is a "Show advanced details" toggle in the Inspector — single-component change, low cost. **NOT** in v1 scope.
- **Phase 12 / DEC-026 stays untouched.** Phase 13 does NOT redefine, defer-further, or pull-forward any of Phase 12's static-precision menu items. After Phase 13 ships, the operator may revisit Phase 12's deferral list with the benefit of dynamic-trace telemetry — but that is a Phase 12 conversation, not a Phase 13 commitment.

**Risks (per DEC-030):**
- **v2.0 changes are FE-isolated to three files** (`executionFlowGraph.ts` + `ExecutionFlow.tsx` + `App.css`) — revert is a single-commit operation if dogfood surfaces a regression. No schema changes, no new endpoints, no new deps — the rollback surface is byte-equal to the v1 ship modulo the three touched files.
- **v2.0-tests adds 2-3 new devDependencies** — the supply-chain risk is small for established dependencies (vitest is widely used, MIT-licensed, well-audited) but non-zero; the v2.0-tests.0 planning checkpoint will explicitly bound the devDep delta as part of Q5 (bundle ceiling = "production bundle must be unchanged").
- **v2.1's call-graph edge injection is the heavier-blast-radius v2 sub-step** — new BE route + new FE edge variant + potentially-deep changes to `executionFlowGraph.ts`'s BFS layer. Operator-demand-gating reduces the risk of premature commitment; the v2.0 ship buys us dogfood time to measure whether v2.0's lightweight wins (column-rank entry-first sort + visual cleanup) are sufficient.
- **`fill: context-stroke` browser-compat floor** is Chrome 99+ / Firefox 90+ / Safari 16+ — AndroScan's desktop-pentester browser target sits comfortably above this floor; the fallback to a non-rendering arrowhead in older browsers is the same as v1's broken state (no regression in older browsers, win in newer).
- **DEC-029's three load-bearing v1 locks** (color-only edge emphasis, three-mode Static/Dynamic/Both toggle, threshold-color hook-count ladder ≤20/21-50/51-100/>100) stay UNTOUCHED by v2. Future contributors should NOT re-thicken fired edges, NOT collapse the three-mode toggle, NOT re-band the threshold ladder without explicit operator sign-off + a new DEC entry. The v2 iteration is strictly additive on top of v1, not a replacement of v1.

two new `useCallback` handlers `onRestart` (if `running`, `await stop()` then `void start({entry, hops})` — the hook's `start()` already wipes state to `INITIAL_DYNAMIC_TRACE_STATE` before re-arming so no explicit `reset()` is needed between stop + start) + `onClear` (just `reset()`); new visibility flags `hasRuntimeState = firedMethods.size > 0 || liveValues.size > 0 || summaries.size > 0 || hookFailed.size > 0` + `showRestart = (isRunning || hasRuntimeState) && !isBusy` + `showClear = hasRuntimeState && !isRunning && !isBusy`; two new conditional `<button>` elements rendered between the existing Run/Stop button and the status copy — Restart with warn-orange-on-hover styling (`title="Stop the current trace (if running), forget what fired, and re-run from scratch — useful if you mis-interacted with the Android app mid-trace"`) + Clear with muted-on-default + text-on-hover styling (`title="Forget what fired without re-running — clears fired-method accents, live-value chips, and per-method summaries from the overlay without touching the backend session"`). **`androscan/web/frontend/src/App.css`** (~+25 LOC) — two new rules `.lab-dynamic-trace-button-restart` (border-default + text-default base; `--warn` border + color on hover; matches the `.lab-dynamic-trace-status-warn` warning hue family) + `.lab-dynamic-trace-button-clear` (border-default + `--muted` base; `--text` border + color on hover; tertiary-action restraint) inserted between the existing `.lab-dynamic-trace-button-running` and `.lab-dynamic-trace-status` rules. **Visibility matrix (final):** **idle / no session ever** → no extra buttons (Run/Stop control stays uncluttered). **starting / stopping** → no extra buttons (`isBusy` gate prevents queuing an action on top of in-flight transition). **running, no events yet** → Restart visible (Clear hidden — clearing while events stream in would just re-populate). **running, events fired** → Restart visible (Clear hidden). **stopped, has runtime state** → BOTH Restart + Clear visible (the "post-mortem cleanup" workflow). **stopped, no events** → no extra buttons. **error / disconnected with events** → BOTH visible (same as stopped). **error / disconnected, no events** → no extra buttons. **Verification:** **pytest 1330 → 1330 passed** (zero BE touched); **vitest 29/29 passed** (no new tests — pure UI affordance, no logic surface; the existing `useDynamicTrace.reset` + `.start` + `.stop` semantics they leverage are already covered by the v3.X-next.2 emitter tests + the v3.X-next.1 BE tests); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean (CSS 132.52 → 132.87 KB / **+0.35 KB raw** for the two new button rules + doc comment; JS 1507.38 → 1508.28 KB / **+0.90 KB raw** for the callbacks + button JSX + visibility flags + doc comment). Zero lint impact. **Edge cases handled:** (1) `Restart` during `running` calls `await stop()` first which sends DELETE to the BE — clean session teardown, not a leaked Frida session relying on the BE's session-timeout GC. (2) `Restart` during `stopped` / `error` / `disconnected` skips the `await stop()` branch (since `state.connection !== "running"`) — `stop()` would itself return early if `sessionRef.current` is null, but the explicit guard makes the intent clearer. (3) `reset()` already bumps `genRef` so any in-flight WS messages from before the click are dropped — no stale-event clobber of the new session's state. (4) `state.error` is wiped by `reset()` (which sets state to `INITIAL_DYNAMIC_TRACE_STATE`) — so `Clear overlay` also serves as the recovery path from "WebSocket dropped" / "server error" toasts. **No backend work** — the hook's `reset()` + `start()` + `stop()` were already there from Phase 13 v1 (`useDynamicTrace`); v3.X-next.6 only wires `reset()` into a new operator-clickable affordance. **No new tests** — UI-affordance-only change with no logic surface; the visibility-flag computation is straightforward boolean algebra and the underlying `useDynamicTrace` state machine is already test-covered. **No new dependencies.** **DEC-031** stays Active (closing note already appended at v3.X-next.2; v3.X-next.6 lands under DEC-031's umbrella as a small operator-recovery follow-up — no new decision-tier lock needed since the policy choice ((a) strict vs (b) restart vs (c) both) is implementation-detail, not architectural). **Files (2 touched + 3 docs):** `androscan/web/frontend/src/tabs/LabTraceMode.tsx`, `androscan/web/frontend/src/App.css`, plus `docs/TASKS.md` (this Active Task block + this v3.X-next.6 row marked `[x]`), `docs/STATE.md` (Recent completed work entry + Next expected milestone flipped), `docs/DECISIONS.md` (DEC-031 closing-note follow-up appended). | v3.X-next.2 (dynamic-overlay surface in production) |

**Risks (per DEC-031):**
- **v3.X-next.1 slicer change is additive (Q4=(b))** — old `trace.sqlite` caches deserialise cleanly with empty `method_invocations`; v3.1 emitter ignores the new field; production rendering unchanged at v3.X-next.1's close. Roll-back = single-commit revert of the slicer change.
- **v3.X-next.2 is the bigger blast-radius sub-step** — emitter rewrite + v2-module deletion + URL gate removal + FE test runner introduction land in a single commit. Per N2=(i) immediate-v2-deletion (operator-confirmed pre-live tool, no production operators to break), no straddle commit / migration window / `?flow=v2` rollback affordance. Catastrophic regression path = git revert / point-release. If post-v3.X-next.2 dogfood surfaces a regression, the rollback restores both the v2 module AND the URL gate in one operation.
- **`@dagrejs/dagre@^3.0.0` dependency carry-forward** — already adopted at v3 preview (commit `c59d879`); v3.X-next.2 doesn't add or remove the dep; the JS bundle's ~50 KB Dagre slice is the steady-state cost of CFG-aware layout. Operator already ratified the trade-off at v3 preview ship.
- **FE test runner introduction is itself a load-bearing decision** — the v3.X-next.2 planning checkpoint will explicitly bound the devDep delta as part of Q1 (test runner choice — vitest recommended). Mirror cadence is LCP.0 / Phase 11 v2.1.0 / DEC-027 — one architectural decision per planning checkpoint, no decision-stacking.
- **`method_invocations` gap handling (Q5 at v3.X-next.2 planning)** — slicer can't always surface a CallSite for cross-module invoke targets; the emitter needs a graceful fallback. Recommendation (a) fall-back-to-v3.1-behaviour-for-that-method preserves the empty-dict-renders-as-current-rendering invariant from Q4=(b). Hybrid (c) wires `call_graph.sqlite::neighbors()` as recovery (subsumes the original v2.1 call-graph-injection scope per DEC-031 diagnosis).
- **Deferred chip-click behaviour** — re-open after v3.X-next.2 lands; the choice between (a) multi-line highlight in Code Browser / (b) popover list with predicate snippets / (c) hybrid is operator-call once they've dogfooded the CFG-aware ranking. Tracked as a v3.X-next.3 candidate; not committed.
- **DEC-029's three load-bearing v1 locks** (color-only edge emphasis, three-mode Static/Dynamic/Both toggle, threshold-color hook-count ladder ≤20/21-50/51-100/>100) + **DEC-030's v2.0 lightweight wins** (global sink coalescing, `fill: context-stroke`, within-page fullscreen, entry-first sort) stay UNTOUCHED by v3.X-next. Future contributors should NOT regress these without explicit operator sign-off + new DEC entries.

**§ LCP — llama.cpp local provider — sub-step backlog** *(strictly linear; one sub-step per Agent-mode session; ratified in LCP.0 2026-05-06 — DEC-027; parallel infrastructure track on `main`, not a Phase, closest precedent is CI.0; orthogonal to Phase 12 v3 work — both can be Active simultaneously)*

The LCP track swaps the local LLM runtime from Ollama (`qwen3.5:35b` heavy on a 36 GB M3 unified-memory budget per the operator's 2026-05-05 status share) to **llama.cpp** (specifically `llama-server`'s OpenAI-compatible HTTP shim). Operator runs `llama-server -c 16384 -ngl 99 -fa --port 8033 --host 127.0.0.1 --jinja` against an UD-Q5_K_XL / Q5_K_M Qwen3-family GGUF; AndroScan consumes `http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1/chat/completions` via a new dedicated `_call_llamacpp()` HTTP path parallel to `_call_ollama()`. Track ratifies a `CLOUD_PROVIDERS` → top-level `LLM_PROVIDERS` table refactor with `local` / `cloud` sub-sections, a Settings UI top-level radio restructure ("Local (Ollama)" / "Local (llama.cpp)" / "Cloud"), JSON-mode parity baseline (`response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` mirroring Ollama's `format: "json"`), and a committed GBNF grammar enforcement follow-up at LCP.6 benefiting both local providers (Ollama supports JSON-schema mode in newer builds). **Locked decisions per DEC-027 Q1-Q5:** Q1 (B + table refactor) — dedicated `_call_llamacpp()` codepath using `requests` directly (NOT extending `_call_cloud()`'s `openai` SDK path); Q2 (a) — defer GBNF to LCP.6 but **promote LCP.6 from optional → committed** follow-up; Q3 (B) — top-level radio with nested cloud dropdown; Q4 (A) — default `base_url: http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1` (loopback-explicit, port `8033` operator-chosen non-default); Q5 (C) — README pointer only at LCP.5, no `docs/LLAMACPP.md`. **Branch posture:** ship on `main` with selective-staging discipline (rejected feature-branch alternative — operator chose the established Phase 10 / 11 / v2.1 sub-step-by-sub-step cadence after `main` became quiescent at HEAD `15e7c1a` post-v2.1.6). **Explicitly NOT in LCP scope:** OpenAI-native `tools` / `tool_calls` migration (would require rewriting the `skill_requests` JSON convention in `parser.py` + `prompts.py` + every skill handler — large blast radius for no operator-visible value since JSON mode already works); llama.cpp embeddings (operator already uses `fastembed` for lane-1 RAG; second `llama-server` instance for embeddings is operator-demand-gated, not v1); `tool_choice` schema-driven dispatch (deferred to LCP.6 alongside GBNF — both are JSON-validity hardening tools that compose); auto-spawn of `llama-server` as a managed sub-process (operator runs `llama-server` themselves, AndroScan only consumes the HTTP endpoint — same posture as the existing Ollama integration).

| LCP.2 | **[x] `_complete_llamacpp()` HTTP path + router branch + JSON-mode parity + `<think>` strip** *(landed 2026-05-06 commit `fc33658` — backend behavior addition; Ollama path untouched; +34 tests in new `tests/test_llm_client.py`)* | **Backend.** `androscan/llm/client.py`: new `_call_llamacpp(prompt, *, system_prompt, response_format, on_token, on_thinking, config, ...) -> CompleteResult` function (~80-120 LOC, parallel structure to `_call_ollama()` — body builder for OpenAI-compat `/v1/chat/completions` with `messages` + `temperature` + `max_tokens` + `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` when `response_format=="json"`; SSE streaming via `requests.post(..., stream=True)` + `iter_lines()` parsing OpenAI's `data: {...}` chunks; retry logic on transient HTTP errors). The router in `complete()` becomes a three-way switch on `Config.provider_kind()`: `"local-ollama"` → existing `_call_ollama()`; `"local-openai-compat"` → new `_call_llamacpp()`; `"cloud"` → existing `_call_cloud()`. `<think>...</think>` strip on the response `content` field (defensive — Qwen3-family reasoning models can leak think-blocks into the content channel depending on the build's `--reasoning-format` flag; the existing parser in `androscan/llm/parser.py` should still find clean JSON after the strip). New helper `_strip_think_blocks(text: str) -> tuple[str, str]` returns `(content_without_think, extracted_thinking)`; both `_call_ollama()` and `_call_llamacpp()` call it for parity. Existing `is_ollama_available()` becomes one of two health-check helpers (LCP.3 adds `is_llamacpp_available()`); the existing function stays unchanged for backwards-compat. **Tests** new file `tests/test_llm_client.py` (~+8-10): request body shape (messages with system + user; `temperature` + `max_tokens` + `response_format`); SSE stream parsing happy path; retry on transient HTTP errors; `<think>` strip happy path + idempotency on no-think content; max_tokens mapping from `Config.ollama_num_predict` (yes — the field name stays `ollama_num_predict` in v1; LCP.4 adds parallel `llamacpp_*` fields if needed). Verification: full pytest suite green; `tsc --noEmit` clean (no FE changes); operators with `llm_provider="ollama"` see no behavior change. | LCP.1 |

| LCP.6 | **[x] GBNF grammar enforcement for `skill_requests` / `hypotheses` schema** *(landed 2026-05-06 — single commit, no 6a/6b split; +62 tests; closes ISSUE-016)* | Strict-committed follow-up per DEC-027 Q2 lock-in shipped. **As-built shape.** New `androscan/llm/grammar.py` module (~+330 LOC including doc-blocks; ~+115 LOC pure-Python) exposing `active_skill_names()` (registry-driven, sorted, deterministic; reads `androscan.skills.list_llm_skills()`), `build_response_json_schema(skill_names)` (Ollama 0.5.0+ `format: <schema>` payload) + `build_response_gbnf(skill_names)` (llama.cpp `grammar:` field on `/v1/chat/completions`; hand-rolled GBNF on top of the standard JSON-value prelude lifted from `llama.cpp/grammars/json.gbnf`), and `is_grammar_enabled(config)` feature-flag helper. Schema shape mirrors `LLMResponse`: `summary` optional string; `skill_requests[*].skill` discriminated-union enum over the 9 LLM-tier skills; `skill_requests[*].params` permissive object (per-skill `params_schema` is operator-prose, not typed JSON Schema, so no per-skill discriminated union); `hypotheses[*]` permissive object (`additionalProperties: True`, no required fields — matches the parser's fail-soft default-fill posture). Top-level `additionalProperties: False`. **Wiring.** `androscan/llm/client.py` extended with per-base-url disabled-set caches (`_OLLAMA_SCHEMA_DISABLED_URLS`, `_LLAMACPP_GRAMMAR_DISABLED_URLS`) + narrow heuristics `_looks_like_schema_error` / `_looks_like_grammar_error` for the opportunistic 400-fallback. `_complete_ollama` swaps `format: "json"` → `format: <schema-dict>` when `Config.local_grammar_enabled` is True and base_url isn't cached; `_complete_llamacpp` attaches `grammar: <gbnf-source>` ALONGSIDE the existing `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}`. On HTTP 400 matching the heuristic, base_url is cached and the request retries within the same loop iteration with the v1-LCP wire shape. Caches are process-lifetime; cloud path unchanged. **Config.** New `local_grammar_enabled: bool = True` field — YAML `llm.local_grammar_enabled`, env `ANDROSCAN_LOCAL_GRAMMAR_ENABLED` (truthy/falsy aliases `true`/`1`/`yes`/`on` ↔ `false`/`0`/`no`/`off`). Live-reloadable. Wired through every loader path (`Config.default()`, `_merge_from_yaml`, env overrides, `with_overrides`, `coerce_yaml_value`). **Settings UI.** `LlmProviderRadio` filter excludes the new field from the Cloud subsection's `llmCloudFields` and renders it under BOTH the "Local (Ollama)" and "Local (llama.cpp)" subsections (cross-local-provider knob; cloud providers ignore it because the OpenAI-compat `response_format` enforces validity at the SDK layer). No new CSS — reuses the existing generic `SettingsField` renderer. **Tests.** 38 in new `tests/test_grammar.py` (skill discovery + JSON-schema shape + GBNF well-formedness + parser-invariance + feature-flag) + 11 in `tests/test_llm_client.py` (Ollama schema-mode happy / 400-fallback / kill-switch off / unrelated-400 / cache-pre-seeded / `response_format=None` skip; llama.cpp grammar-mode happy / 400-fallback / kill-switch off / unrelated-400 / `response_format=None` skip — with `_capturing_post` deep-copy idiom for the dict-mutation snapshot trap) + 13 in `tests/test_config.py::TestLocalGrammarEnabled`. **6a/6b split decision: single commit, no split.** The LCP.6 planning checkpoint surveyed `SkillMeta.params_schema` across all 9 LLM-tier skills and confirmed it's operator-prose (not typed JSON Schema), so the LCP.0 emitter-scope estimate was over-scoped (~5x); the actual emitter is small enough for a single commit. **Verification:** all 1092 tests pass (1030 → 1092, +62), `tsc --noEmit` clean, `vite build` clean (1235.53 KB JS / 384.66 KB gzip / 93.42 KB CSS / 15.71 KB gzip). **Closes `ISSUE-016`** — moved to the Resolved section of `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md`. **Operator impact:** Q4_K_M / IQ4_XS local-LLM quants regain reliable structured-JSON output — the model's logits-sampler can ONLY emit registered skill names + integers in the 1-5 range; off-vocab / off-range output is impossible at the sampling level. | LCP.5 |
| LCP.7 | **[ ] Model-sourcing UX (Settings UI) — planning checkpoint pending** *(opened 2026-05-06 alongside DEC-028; sub-step planning checkpoint pending — no commit until LCP.7.0 ratifies the open questions)* | **Spec stub (operator-agreed at DEC-028 ratification).** `llama.cpp` model sourcing differs from Ollama's single `ollama pull`-managed registry — three orthogonal sources need a per-mode UX in Settings → Global → LLM provider → Local (llama.cpp): **(M1)** HuggingFace cache (operator already downloaded the GGUF; `llama-server -cl 2>/dev/null` lists cache entries); **(M2)** HuggingFace Hub (operator passes `-hf <repo>:<quant>`; `llama-server` downloads on first run; private repos require `HF_TOKEN` env var); **(M3)** Local filesystem (operator passes `-m /absolute/path/to/foo.gguf`). **Initial design sketch (NOT ratified):** 3-way radio "HF cache / HF Hub / Local file" inside the existing "Local (llama.cpp)" subsection; each radio choice swaps the input widget below it (text-search dropdown for M1 populated from `llama-server -cl` via a new `/api/llamacpp/cached-models` backend route; free-text `<repo>:<quant>` for M2 with optional `HF_TOKEN` warning pill when private-repo syntax detected; file-path picker for M3 — likely a styled text input with a "Browse" button that opens an OS file picker via the existing approach used elsewhere in the app); the existing `default_model` text input acts as a free-text fallback when the operator types in a label that doesn't match any of the 3 radio paths (preserves backwards-compat for operators already running with a custom label). **Open questions (LCP.7.0 planning checkpoint must answer):** Q1 — How does AndroScan acquire the cached-models list at runtime? `llama-server -cl` is a CLI subcommand on the operator's box, not an HTTP endpoint on the running server (which serves `/v1/models` for the LOADED model only, not the cached set). Options: (a) shell out to `llama-server -cl` from the AndroScan backend on demand, (b) polling on a timer, (c) operator-triggered "Refresh cache list" button. Q2 — How does AndroScan handle `HF_TOKEN` for private HF repos? Operator-agreed at DEC-028 ratification: HF Hub mode requires a `HF_TOKEN` to download — surface as an opt-in textbox in the M2 sub-section with the usual API-key handling caveats; out of scope for v1 if it adds material plumbing complexity, defer to a later sub-step. Q3 — Does AndroScan show the operator that LCP.7 model selection is **advisory** (just sets the `default_model` label for log readability) and DOES NOT change which GGUF llama-server actually loads (that's set at server startup via `-m` / `-hf`)? Strong yes — the docstring on the section needs to be explicit. Q4 — File-path picker for M3 — implement via the OS file picker, or a styled text input with operator-typed absolute path? OS file picker is friendlier but adds frontend complexity. Q5 — Settings UI bundle ceiling carry-over from LCP.0? +5 KB JS / +2 KB CSS still applies (LCP.7 is part of LCP track). Q6 — Test-count target? Likely +15-25 tests (3 new backend route tests for `/api/llamacpp/cached-models` + frontend wire-shape tests + per-mode submit-validation tests). **No code lands until LCP.7.0 planning checkpoint locks Q1-Q6.** | LCP.6 + DEC-028 |
| LCP.8 | **[ ] Chat-dock SSE provider routing — planning checkpoint pending** *(opened 2026-05-21 by operator dogfood under llama.cpp on the Reports tab chat dock; ISSUE-023 carries the diagnosis; **real regression introduced at LCP.2 commit `fc33658`** when the `complete()` router landed without a parallel `stream_complete()` router; closes the LCP-track-was-supposed-to-be-finished hole)* | **Spec stub (ISSUE-023; planning checkpoint pending).** **Root cause.** LCP.2 introduced a provider-aware router in `androscan/llm/client.py::complete()` (line 1056-1106) that calls `config.provider_kind()` and dispatches to `_call_ollama` / `_call_llamacpp` / `_call_cloud`. The parallel `stream_complete()` helper (line 295-355) — used by the workbench chat SSE endpoint via `androscan/web/chat.py:631-634` — was NOT made provider-aware. It stays hard-coded to the Ollama `/api/chat` endpoint reading `config.ollama_base_url` / `config.ollama_model` directly. The docstring at line 312-314 admits the carve-out explicitly: *"Cloud providers don't currently flow through this helper; the workbench SSE endpoint is Ollama-only by design (matches the streaming/thinking semantics of the local model)."* That was accurate pre-LCP (cloud providers were the only alternative and have different streaming semantics) but became wrong the moment LCP.2 shipped llama.cpp as a second LOCAL provider. **Operator impact.** Chat dock completely unusable for any operator on `llm.provider: "llamacpp"` — error message is misleadingly `Cannot connect to Ollama at http://localhost:11434` even when the operator's llama.cpp setup is correct + the Status panel's LLM card reads green. Every chat-dependent feature breaks: Ask AI tier-2 path (Phase 11 v2.1.4); "Discuss in chat" on `<Inspector>` (Phase 13 v1 13.9); cross-tab chat affordances on Reports + Inspect findings (Phase 6+); `<TraceEntryCandidateWidget>` agentic-loop substrate (Phase 11 v2.1.5); `<MethodSummaryWidget>` chat-card flow (Phase 13 v1 13.9). Non-streaming paths (every LLM-tier skill — `trace_behavior`, `summarise_method`, `suggest_trace_entry`, `generate_frida_hook`, `query_call_graph`, `search_decompiled_sources`) all flow through `complete()` and route correctly to llama.cpp, so static analysis + skill-based widgets keep working — only the chat dock SSE path is broken. The Settings → Status panel's LLM card runs only `GET /health` + `GET /v1/models` (per `androscan/web/health_probes.py::probe_llamacpp`) so the bug is invisible to operators using Status as a smoke test before chat. **Recommended fix (ratify at LCP.8.0).** Option (a) — add sibling `_stream_llamacpp(prompt, *, system_prompt, response_format, on_token, on_thinking, config, ...) -> CompleteResult` that POSTs to `{base_url}/v1/chat/completions` with `stream: true`, parses the OpenAI-style `data: {...}` SSE chunks, and surfaces `on_token` / `on_thinking` from each chunk's `delta.content` field (parsing well-documented and the existing `_call_cloud()` may already have OpenAI-compat SSE parsing to reuse via the `openai` SDK). Wrap `stream_complete()` in a `provider_kind()`-aware router with the same three-way switch shape `complete()` uses. **Recommended option** — preserves streaming UX on llama.cpp, llama.cpp's OpenAI-compat shim supports streaming natively. ~+150 LOC client + ~+15 tests in `tests/test_llm_client.py` (request-body shape + SSE chunk parsing + `<think>` strip parity + transient HTTP retry). Option (b) — fall back to non-streaming `complete()` from `chat.py` when `provider_kind() == "local-openai-compat"`; ~30 LOC FE change but loses streaming UX on llama.cpp (meaningful UX regression for long chat replies + the agentic-loop substrate). Avoid unless option (a)'s SSE parsing is unexpectedly difficult. **Open questions (LCP.8.0 planning checkpoint must answer):** Q1 — Option (a) full `_stream_llamacpp()` vs option (b) fall-back-to-non-streaming. Recommendation (a). Q2 — Cloud streaming path (currently also routed through `stream_complete()`'s Ollama-only carve-out, though no cloud operators have reported this bug because the chat dock has been Ollama-default since DEC-027) — fold into LCP.8 scope as a third router arm, or carve out as a separate follow-up? Q3 — Extend `probe_llamacpp` to optionally exercise a tiny `/v1/chat/completions` round-trip (~3-token "ping" → "pong" call) so the Status card surfaces chat-path liveness, not just liveness + model-list — part of LCP.8 or separate sub-step? Q4 — Reuse the existing `_strip_think_blocks` helper from LCP.2's non-streaming path for `<think>` block stripping on the streaming path; should `on_thinking` receive raw think-block content (current Ollama posture) or post-stripped clean content? Q5 — Test-count target: ~+15-20 (request-body shape + SSE chunk parsing + happy / 400-fallback / kill-switch / unrelated-400 / `response_format=None` skip + `<think>` strip parity + transient HTTP retry). **No code lands until LCP.8.0 planning checkpoint locks Q1-Q5.** | LCP.7 + DEC-027 (`stream_complete()` provider routing gap from LCP.2 ship) + ISSUE-023 |

**Risks (per DEC-027):**
- **JSON-validity drift on aggressive quants (Q4_K_M / IQ4_XS):** ~~v1 ships `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` parity with Ollama — strictness is "output is valid JSON object," NOT "output matches the `skill_requests`/`hypotheses` schema."~~ **Resolved at LCP.6** (2026-05-06) — GBNF grammar enforcement for llama.cpp + JSON-schema mode for Ollama 0.5.0+ now constrain output at the model's logits-sampling level; off-vocab skill names + off-range integer fields are impossible. Operators on older Ollama (< 0.5.0) or `llama-server` builds without `grammar:` field support transparently fall back to `response_format`-only via the per-base-url 400-fallback cache. Operators wanting to disable grammar enforcement (e.g. for benchmarking or sampling failures) can set `llm.local_grammar_enabled: false` in `global_config.yaml` or `ANDROSCAN_LOCAL_GRAMMAR_ENABLED=false` in env. See `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` ISSUE-016 (Resolved section).
- **LCP.4 Settings UI restructure carries non-trivial main-branch rollback cost.** Operator-visible the moment it ships (radio replaces flat dropdown). Mitigated by the 4a/4b split if the diff balloons (4a ships the UI restructure with Ollama defaults preserved — pre-existing operators see new radio shape but identical knob behavior; 4b ships the llama.cpp sub-knobs — only operator-visible when "Local (llama.cpp)" radio is selected; revert 4b leaves 4a intact as a cheap rollback story).
- **Performance characteristic differences between Ollama and llama.cpp on the same hardware:** llama.cpp's tighter Metal acceleration on M-series typically beats Ollama's perf at the same Q-level by ~10-30% in tokens/sec; flash attention adds another 10-15% on long contexts. Operators switching from Ollama-q4 to llamacpp-Q5_K_M may see net higher quality + comparable speed. LCP track doesn't ratify a perf measurement target — operator runs whatever they want and reports back.
- **OpenAI-native `tools` / `tool_calls` migration deferred indefinitely:** AndroScan's "tool calling" stays as JSON-mode-driven prompt orchestration over the `skill_requests` convention. If a downstream use case ever justifies the migration (e.g. very long agentic chains where per-call event boundaries become operator-visible), capture as a long-tail follow-up.
- **Embeddings via llama.cpp deferred:** operator currently uses `fastembed` for lane-1 RAG. A second `llama-server` instance for embeddings is operator-demand-gated, not v1.
- **Phase 12 v3 work runs in parallel without scheduling conflict:** LCP and Phase 12 are orthogonal layers (infrastructure vs. feature). Both can be Active simultaneously. Conflict surface on shared files is tiny: `docs/STATE.md` + `docs/TASKS.md` Active Task block are append-newer-on-top by convention.

**§ Phase 4 CI — sub-step backlog** *(parallel non-blocking thread per DEC-025; lands before Phase 11 11.4 if scheduling permits, but unblocked otherwise)*

Phase 4 CI was originally parked from the 2026-03 Phase 4 partial scope (error handling + docs cleanup landed; CI deferred). DEC-025 promotes it to an Active Task line because the Phase 11 slicer changes are the largest behavioural change of v2 and warrant a regression safety net before shipping. The work is small, standalone, and independent of Phase 11 — it can land any time during 11.x, but sequencing it before 11.4 is the better calendar.

| #     | Title | Deliverable |
|-------|-------|-------------|
| CI.0  | **Phase 4 CI — pytest on push/PR** *(non-blocking, can land any time during 11.x; sequence before 11.4 if possible)* | New `.github/workflows/test.yml` running the full pytest suite on `push` to `main` + `pull_request` against `main`. Python 3.11+ matrix (matches local dev — qwen3.5:35b operator setup runs against Python 3.11 per `pyproject.toml`'s `requires-python`). Caches pip + the apktool / jadx tarballs the slow tests use (per the `tests/test_first_run_setup.py` install-once pattern). Skips `device`-marked tests by default (`pytest -m "not device"` matches the local CI default; opt-in marker registered in DEC-023 sub-step 4.3). No frontend tests yet (no FE test runner per project convention; `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` are still hand-run gates — adding them to CI is a separate v3 follow-up if FE-test demand surfaces). Locks in the **~750-test floor as a regression safety net** before Phase 11's slicer changes ship in 11.4. |

**Risks:**
- GitHub Actions minutes cost: cached pip + apktool/jadx tarballs keep cost bounded; the suite runs in ~3–5 min on a `ubuntu-latest` runner per local profiling. Not a meaningful ops cost.
- Default-suite skips `device`-marked tests, so a Frida-touching regression won't surface in CI — same posture as the local default (`pytest -m "not device"`); the operator gate for device runs is unchanged.

---

### Phase 6 — Web UI shell with emulator mirror

**Goal:** Serve a React frontend from a FastAPI backend with live emulator screen mirroring and basic project browsing.

1. **Backend skeleton** (`androscan/web/`): FastAPI app with CORS, static file serving, WebSocket endpoints; `GET /api/projects` (list `apps/` folders), `GET /api/projects/{app_id}/runs`, `GET /api/dossier/{app_id}/{run_ts}`, `GET /api/findings/{app_id}/{run_ts}`; config keys `web_host`, `web_port` in `global_config.yaml`.
2. **React frontend scaffold** (`androscan/web/frontend/`): Vite + React + TypeScript; layout — sidebar (projects, dossier components, findings), main area (mirror + detail panels); dark theme, monospace code areas.
3. **Emulator screen mirror:** prefer `scrcpy` piped to WebSocket (MJPEG/h264) or `adb exec-out screencap` polling fallback; `<canvas>` or `<video>` in React; touch forwarding via `adb shell input tap x y`.
4. **Live logcat streaming:** backend `adb logcat` subprocess → WebSocket; frontend scrollable panel with level coloring; filter by tag/level/keyword.
5. **CLI integration:** e.g. `python androscan.py --serve [--port …]` starts server; optional `python androscan.py --apk … --serve` runs analysis then opens UI.
6. **Tests:** unit tests for REST responses (mock data); WebSocket connect/disconnect lifecycle.
7. **Dependencies (target):** Python `fastapi`, `uvicorn[standard]`, `websockets`; frontend `react`, `react-dom`, `vite`, `typescript`.

---

### Phase 7 — Click-to-code mapping

**Goal:** Tap on mirrored screen → identify UI element → trace to Activity/class/method → show decompiled source in Monaco.

1. **UI element identification:** on tap, `uiautomator dump` (or equivalent); parse XML; match coordinates to bounds; extract `resource-id`, `class`, `content-desc`, `text`, `package`, `bounds`.
2. **Activity resolution:** `dumpsys activity top` (or equivalent) for foreground Activity.
3. **Resource ID → source:** parse `R.java` / `R$id.java` from jadx output; search for `findViewById(R.id.…)` / View Binding; resolve class + method.
4. **Monaco Editor:** `@monaco-editor/react` in detail panel; Java/Kotlin highlighting; read-only default; jump to line for resource reference.
5. **API:** `POST /api/tap` (x, y) → element + source mapping; `GET /api/source/{class_name}` (delegate to existing jadx-backed decompilation path).
6. **LLM-assisted mapping (stretch):** when deterministic trace fails, optional LLM hint with reasoning shown alongside deterministic result.
7. **Tests:** unit — XML parsing, coordinate matching, R.java parsing; integration — tap → element → source with fixtures. **[done]** `tests/test_inspect_map.py` covers element pick + handler grep + adb glue; `tests/test_web.py::test_api_inspect_map_returns_element_and_candidates` covers the end-to-end `/api/inspect/map` response (including the new `resolution` block); `tests/test_resolve_ui_element.py` covers the fuser scorer + RAG enrichment + skill registry path.
8. **[done]** New skill `resolve_ui_element` (**llm** tier): takes element + foreground activity + raw handler candidates; scores them deterministically (kind base + foreground-activity bonus + activity-named-file bonus + early-line decay) and optionally enriches with Lane-1 RAG hits; returns `{best, alternatives, rag_hits, reasoning}`. Pure-function `resolve()` is also called inline by `/api/inspect/map`.

---

### Phase 8 — Static call graph from Smali

**Goal:** Build navigable call graph from decoded Smali; visualize with Cytoscape.js; support LLM-assisted path questions.

1. **Smali analysis** (`androscan/analysis/call_graph.py` or equivalent): walk apktool output; extract classes, methods, `invoke-*` targets; adjacency caller.method → callees; `extends` / `implements`.
2. **Graph storage:** `apps/<app_id>/call_graph.json` (nodes + edges); rebuild when APK hash changes; node metadata (exported flag from dossier where applicable).
3. **Graph query API:** `GET /api/graph/{app_id}` (paginated/filtered); `GET /api/graph/{app_id}/neighbors/{class.method}`; `GET /api/graph/{app_id}/paths?from=…&to=…`; `POST /api/graph/{app_id}/query` (LLM-assisted natural-language path questions).
4. **Cytoscape.js:** zoom/pan/search/filter; click node → Monaco source; highlight paths; layouts (e.g. dagre, cose-bilkent).
5. **New skill (target):** `query_call_graph` (**llm** tier): question + graph subset → relevant paths; usable from analysis workflow.
6. **Tests:** unit — fixture Smali, graph build, path algorithms; integration — APK → graph → query (where feasible without device).

---

### Phase 9 — Frida integration (live tracing & hooking)

**Goal:** Dynamic instrumentation from the UI — hook methods, trace calls, optional return tampering; LLM-assisted hook generation.

1. **Frida adapter** (`androscan/adapters/frida_client.py` or equivalent): detect/push frida-server (configurable); Python `frida` attach/load/detach; availability checks like other external tools.
2. **Hook templates** (`androscan/adapters/frida_hooks/`): parameterized JS — method entry/exit log, SSL pinning bypass patterns, crypto/SharedPreferences/Intent hooks; render from class/method/signature from graph or dossier.
3. **Live trace streaming:** Frida `message` → WebSocket → UI trace panel (pause/resume, search, export).
4. **Interactive hook builder:** pick method from graph or editor → hook type (log args, log return, modify return, custom JS); optional LLM “generate hook for objective”; **user confirm before deploy**.
5. **New skill (target):** `generate_frida_hook` (**llm** tier): class, method, objective → Frida JS; basic syntax validation before deploy.
6. **Exploit verification integration (optional):** extend signal matrix (`vuln_module_skills_signals.json`) with e.g. `frida_trace` where product requires deeper runtime evidence.
7. **Tests:** unit — template rendering, JS generation, adapter with mocks; device integration opt-in only.
8. **Dependencies (target):** `frida`, `frida-tools`; frida-server on device (operator-managed).

**Cross-cutting:** artifacts under `apps/<app_id>/` (graphs, traces, hooks); new skills follow three-tier model; web server **bind 127.0.0.1** by default (local single-user); `global_config.yaml` sections `web:` and `frida:` (when implemented).

---

### Phase 10 — Behavior Trace (Lab tab gate-identification mode)

**Goal:** From a UI element or method anchor, enumerate the closure of conditional gates that govern its behaviour, classify each gate's outcome, and propose template-bound bypass plans the operator can stage → inject via the existing Hook Lab v1 Stage→Inject path. Trace becomes the headline mode of the **Lab** tab (renamed from Hook Lab per DEC-024); the existing Cytoscape pane is demoted to "Graph mode" alongside `Manual Hooks` and `Trace`.

**Workflow:** *Anchor → Locate → Trace → Classify → Manipulate → Verify*. Operator picks an anchor (Mirror tap → click-to-code resolution from Phase 7 / DEC-019, or a method node from the Lab Graph mode); the static layer enumerates branches in the forward closure (≤ 3 hops by default, hard-capped at 6, methods-cap at 30); the LLM is invoked once per anchor to interpret and propose plans; the operator stages a plan via the existing `HookBuilder.tsx` flow (which is now Lab's `Manual Hooks` mode) and injects via the existing `POST /api/frida/sessions` allowlist-gated path; verification is operator-driven (re-tap the anchor, watch the Frida overlay light up the same gate methods in cyan).

**Deliverables (target):**

- New module `androscan/analysis/decisions.py` (decision-point extraction over the Smali instruction stream); new `androscan/analysis/slicing.py` (intra-procedural backward slicing for predicate origin); new `androscan/analysis/branch_classifier.py` (deterministic deny/allow/neutral classifier with a confidence float); new `androscan/analysis/bypass_planner.py` (template-bound plan synthesis with `low/medium/high` risk taxonomy); new `androscan/analysis/trace_types.py` (platform-neutral data model — `BehaviorAnchor`, `DecisionPoint`, `BypassPlan`, `MethodRef`, `FieldRef`).
- New LLM-tier skill `trace_behavior` (`requires_confirmation=False`; per-anchor LLM call only — per-decision rejected per DEC-024); per-app SQLite cache at `apps/<app_id>/.decompiled/<sha>/trace.sqlite` (schema_version 1, mirrors call-graph / RAG storage layout).
- New REST routes `GET / POST / DELETE /api/trace/{app_id}/anchor`; new `androscan/web/trace_routes.py` factory-built and wired into `androscan/web/app.py`.
- New frontend Trace mode under `androscan/web/frontend/src/components/trace/` (anchor card, decision timeline with verdict badges, bypass plan cards with "Stage in Manual Hooks" buttons, truncated / incomplete banners); 3-mode left-rail switcher in Lab (`Trace | Manual Hooks | Graph`) with Trace as the default; Inspect → Trace cross-tab plumbing via `pendingTraceEntry`; new `trace` chat-attachment kind; back-compat `#/hook` → `#/lab` URL hash redirect.
- Hook Lab → Lab code rename (`HookLabTab.tsx` → `LabTab.tsx`, `HookLabCodeView` → `LabCodeView`, per-tab chat-prompt key in `androscan/web/chat.py`, `frontend/README.md` reference) — all in sub-step 10.6 alongside the tab routing changes.

**Tests:** ~80 new tests across Phase 10 (mostly `tests/test_decisions_*.py`, `tests/test_branch_classifier.py`, `tests/test_bypass_planner.py`, `tests/test_trace_behavior_skill.py`, `tests/test_trace_routes.py`); deterministic fixture-driven static analysis; LLM mocked at the test boundary; no device touching in default suite (the `device` pytest marker from DEC-023 sub-step 4.3 covers any opt-in cases).

**Full sub-task checklist:** § Phase 10 — Behavior Trace v1 — sub-step backlog above (10.0 → 10.8). Locked in **DEC-024**.
