## Completed Tasks

### 2026-04-29 Phase 10 (sub-step 10.8): Mirror → Trace integration + chat hook + docs sweep — Phase 10 v1 complete
- outcome: The wrap-up that closes the Phase 10 loop on the operator surface — completes the Inspect → Trace cross-tab handoff, surfaces the active `BehaviorAnchor` to the Lab chat dock as a new attachment kind, and lands the Phase 10 docs sweep across the six canonical files. **Mirror → Trace cross-tab handoff.** New "trace ↗" button on the Inspect tab's `BestBanner` (rendered only when the new `onTraceBehaviour` prop is supplied — `BestBanner` stays a pure presentational component). The button hands the fuser's `ResolutionCandidate` back to `InspectTab.handleTraceBehaviour`, which converts the picked file path into a Smali class descriptor via the new `javaRelPathToSmaliClass(relPath)` utility (`com/example/Foo.java` → `Lcom/example/Foo;`) and combines it with `best.method_name` (when present — only RAG candidates carry it) via `javaRelPathToSmaliMethodPrefix(relPath, methodName)` to produce a *prefix* (`Lcom/example/Foo;->onClick(` when method known, else `Lcom/example/Foo;->`); the descriptor list is intentionally left for the operator to fill in since the deterministic resolver doesn't carry per-overload parameter descriptors. Handler then writes `setPendingTraceEntry({appId, entryPrefix, sourceLabel: "Inspect → <file>#<method>" | "Inspect → <file>:<line>"})`, calls `setLabMode("trace")`, and `setTab("lab")`. **`pendingTraceEntry` plumbing** mirrors `pendingCodeNav` / `pendingHookPrefill` exactly: new `PendingTraceEntryInput` / ts-bumped `PendingTraceEntry` types + `setPendingTraceEntry` callback on `WorkbenchContext`. `LabTraceMode.tsx` reads it on mount + on `(pendingTraceEntry?.ts, appId)` change: prefills draft + hops, sets `seedLabel`, **auto-fires the trace** only when the seeded value is a complete signature (heuristic via `looksLikeCompleteSmaliSignature(s)` — closing paren + recognised return descriptor `^\[*([VZBSCIJFD]|L[\w/$]+;)$`); otherwise just prefills so the operator can finish the descriptor list. The seed label drops on operator edit / Trace fire; `×` button on the pill clears both label and draft. **Lab chat — `trace` `ChatAttachment`.** Cross-mode plumbing: `LabTab.tsx` lifts `activeAnchor: BehaviorAnchor | null`; `LabTraceMode` republishes via a new `onActiveAnchorChange?(anchor | null)` callback; `ManualHooksMode` accepts `activeAnchor` and threads it into both `buildHookChatAttachments` and `buildHookChatContextSummary`. `_renderTraceAttachment(anchor)` emits human-readable plain text — entry-method header, summary line (`hops=N · decisions=M · plans=K (+J advanced) · TRUNCATED · INCOMPLETE`), optional rationale, numbered decision timeline (one line per gate: `class.method @N [kind] branch=verdict(score), … ← <origin> [LOW-CONF]`), and the **top-3 default-tier bypass plans**. Three soft caps: `CHAT_TRACE_BUDGET = 6_000` chars, `CHAT_TRACE_TOP_PLANS = 3`, `CHAT_TRACE_MAX_DECISIONS = 40` (with `+ N more decision(s)` trailer). `buildHookChatContextSummary` extended with a 2-line block (`Active behaviour trace: <class.method> · hops=N · M decision(s) · K plan(s) (+J advanced)` + budget caption). Active anchor cleared via `useEffect([appId])` so a second project never inherits the first's trace context. **Backend.** `androscan/web/chat.py` `ATTACHMENT_BUDGETS["trace"] = 6_000` (matches `code` — same shape, same ceiling). Lab system prompt extended with a sentence telling the model to treat the trace attachment's decision-timeline list as authoritative ground truth and prefer recommending bypass plans rooted in those gates over speculation from the decompiled code alone (alias-aware via `_normalise_tab` so legacy `tab="hook"` requests get the new prompt too). `ChatDock.tsx` `KIND_LABELS["trace"] = "Behavior trace"`. **Tests.** **+4 backend** in `tests/test_chat_guardrails.py` (the `trace` budget key is set + matches `code`; oversized trace truncates at the `trace` budget, not the default 2,000-char cap; under-budget trace passes through verbatim wrapped in `<context kind="trace">`; lab system prompt mentions `trace` and the alias prompt is identical). No new FE tests per project convention. Full suite: **749 / 749 green** (was 745; +4). `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean — 709 modules, 1.20 MB JS / 75 KB CSS / 373 KB gzipped (modest growth from 10.7's 1.19 / 74 / 372 attributable to the trace-attachment renderer + the seed-pill CSS + the cross-tab handler). **Docs sweep landed in this commit:** `docs/STATE.md` (10.8 sub-bullet under the Phase 10 entry + Recent completed work entry; Phase 10 header gets a "v1 complete 2026-04-29" stamp); this `docs/TASKS.md` (Active Task → "Phase 10 — Behavior Trace v1 complete (2026-04-29)" + 10.8 row marked `[x]` + this Completed Tasks entry); `docs/DESIGN_DOC.md` (Phase 10 stub status promoted to "**v1 landed 2026-04-29**" with cross-link to STATE.md); `docs/DECISIONS.md` DEC-024 (Status line gets a `**v1 complete 2026-04-29**` closing note while staying `Active` for the cross-platform follow-ups DEC-024 explicitly defers to v2); `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` (new ISSUE-013 captures the v1 intra-procedural-slicing false-negative posture; new ISSUE-014 captures the per-overload precision gap on `predicate_origin` two-register tie-breaks); `docs/SAFETY_AND_SECURITY.md` §12.7 (new "Trace mode (Phase 10)" subsection — read-only static layer, single per-anchor LLM call, template-bound bypass plans, dynamic effects flow exclusively through the existing Hook Lab v1 Stage→Inject controls + the per-app `hook_target_package_prefix` allowlist). **Final v1 test count for Phase 10:** ~104 new tests across 10.1 → 10.8 (over the 80-test target from DEC-024), spread weighted toward the static layer (10.1 → 10.4 = +77 tests) where determinism makes regression coverage cheap; 10.5 = +22 (LLM mocked at the test boundary), 10.6 = +18 (REST + alias), 10.7 = +0 (no FE test runner per convention), 10.8 = +4 (chat attachment).
- notes: The choice to leave the Smali signature *prefix* incomplete on cross-tab seed (rather than have the backend resolve `(class_name, method_name)` to a list of overload signatures via the call-graph store) preserves the "v1 honest about its limits" posture from DEC-024 — the operator sees exactly what the resolver produced, and a backend overload-resolver would either pick the wrong overload silently (dangerous) or surface a picker (scope creep for 10.8). The descriptor-completion path in v2 should land as an autocomplete affordance in the Trace mode form input itself (typing `Lcom/example/Foo;->onClick(` triggers a backend lookup against the call graph for matching overloads), not as a backend resolver in the cross-tab handoff. The choice to auto-fire only on a complete signature (vs. always firing) preserves operator control — false-positive auto-fires would burn an LLM call per click on partial seeds, defeating the purpose of the "Trace this behaviour" button. The conservative `looksLikeCompleteSmaliSignature` heuristic (closing paren + return descriptor regex match) is intentionally narrow — it's a "yes I'm confident" signal, not a "good enough" check. The decision to lift `activeAnchor` into `LabTab` (rather than into `WorkbenchContext`) keeps the cross-mode coupling local — the chat dock and the trace mode are siblings in the same tab, and `LabTab` already owns the mode-switcher state; bumping it to context would invite the Inspect tab to read it too, which has no use for it. The 6,000-char `trace` budget is precedented by the `code` budget — both attachments are linear human-readable text where 6 KB carries enough signal for the model to reason about a non-trivial method or anchor. The decision to render the trace attachment as plain text (rather than JSON) follows the same precedent as `frida_summary` (also JSON-rendered into the model context) and keeps the model's parsing cheap — JSON-shaped attachments incur tokenisation overhead from the structural punctuation + force the model to track nesting, which doesn't help when the consumer is a chat assistant. Top-3 plans is the right ceiling for chat — operators who want the full ranked list read the Trace UI directly, where rendering is rich + interactive; the chat is for "explain the gates and tell me which plan to try first". The 40-decision per-anchor cap is generous enough for realistic apps (typical anchor closure surfaces 5–20 decisions) while preventing a pathological 200-decision closure from monopolising the budget. The decision to NOT add a cross-tab "Trace this behaviour" button on the Manual Hooks mode's CallGraphView (despite the obvious symmetry with the BestBanner button) is deliberate v2 scope — the call-graph node click already prefills HookBuilder, which is the operator's primary action there; adding a "Trace from here" affordance would dilute that flow and is better landed alongside an in-graph "behavioral marker" overlay (call-graph nodes that have a cached `BehaviorAnchor` get a different render).
- follow-up: **Phase 10 v1 is complete** — the Behavior Trace mode is fully operational across the static layer (10.1–10.4), the LLM-tier skill + per-app cache (10.5), the REST surface + tab shell (10.6), the headline UI (10.7), and the Inspect → Trace cross-tab handoff + chat integration + docs sweep (10.8). DEC-024 stays `Active` for the cross-platform follow-ups it explicitly defers to v2 (iOS / WebView / native binary mirroring, inter-procedural slicing, switch-case bypass plans, per-decision LLM calls, free-form LLM JS via the `generate_frida_hook` operator-driven path). v2 candidates surfacing from Phase 10's v1 ship: (a) backend smali-signature autocomplete in the Trace mode form (typing `Lcom/example/Foo;->onClick(` queries the call graph for matching overloads), (b) cross-tab "Trace from here" button on the Manual Hooks mode's CallGraphView nodes, (c) `BehaviorAnchor`-aware in-graph overlay (cached anchors get a different render), (d) per-app TTL on cached anchors (current invalidation is sha-keyed cache directory + operator `force=true`), (e) operator-configurable confidence-tier thresholds via `trace.length_ratio_*` config keys, (f) inter-procedural slicing for the `ParamOrigin` case (the v1 punt is "method parameter — bypass via the calling site"; v2 walks the calling sites automatically). Next phase to tackle is operator-driven — Phase 4 CI is still parked; Phase 6 polish (scrcpy, richer logcat filtering, RAG attachments in chat) is deferred. Open Hook Lab v1 follow-up: **ISSUE-010** (self-host Monaco from a CDN-fetched bundle) remains v2 — the Trace mode UI stays Monaco-free per design.

### 2026-04-29 Phase 10 (sub-step 10.5): `trace_behavior` LLM-tier skill + per-app `trace.sqlite` cache
- outcome: Fifth code-bearing Phase 10 sub-step and the first one to wire the per-decision static layer (10.1–10.4) into a per-anchor LLM layer. New skill `androscan/skills/trace_behavior.py` (LLM-tier, `requires_confirmation=False` per DEC-022 / DEC-024 — read-only, no device touching, no APK mutation, no Frida injection) registered in `_SKILL_MODULES` (15 → 16 skills). Public API: `execute({"entry_method": <smali_id>, "app_id"?: <id>, "hops"?: <int>, "force"?: <bool>}, context) -> SkillResult`. Pipeline: resolve app/cache directory via the same fallback as `query_call_graph` / `resolve_ui_element`; cache lookup against `apps/<app_id>/.decompiled/<sha>/trace.sqlite` keyed by `(entry_method.smali_signature, hops)` (hit returns immediately with `[cached] ` text prefix); call-graph forward BFS depth ≤ `hops` (clamped to `trace.max_hops_hard_cap`) collecting up to `MAX_TRACE_METHODS = 30` internal callees with truncation flags; per-method `parse_decisions → slice_predicate_origins → classify_branch_outcomes → plan_bypasses` aggregating decisions + plans into the anchor; identify LLM workload (low_confidence_indices = `branch_outcome.confidence < LLM_RECLASSIFY_THRESHOLD = 0.6`; planless_keys = decisions where the deterministic planner returned no plans AND the heuristic outcome had non-zero confidence); **one LLM call per anchor** via `androscan.llm.client.complete(..., response_format="json", stream=False)` with the inline `build_trace_behavior_prompt(...)` emitting a strict JSON-schema system prompt + a deterministic per-decision rendering; re-classification application bumps `BranchOutcome.confidence` to `LLM_RECLASSIFY_CONFIDENCE = 0.75` and appends `"llm-reclassified: <reason>"` to verdict reasons (heuristic score preserved for audit); proposed-plan validation (template_id ∈ `{force_return_value, force_method_skip, force_string_compare_equal}` AND round-trips through `frida_hooks.render_by_id` — invalid plans silently dropped + logged); `partition_by_risk` splits into `default_plans` + `advanced_plans` per `config.trace_bypass_risk_max`; persist via `trace_cache.write_anchor` (INSERT OR REPLACE, idempotent, lazy DB init). **Fail-soft posture (mirrors `query_call_graph`):** missing app context, unbuilt decompile cache, missing call graph, or unresolved entry method → `success=True` with `data=None` and a clear `text` explanation; LLM transport failure / non-JSON response / response-not-an-object → return the deterministic-only payload with `[llm-skipped: <reason>]` in `text` (the static layer alone is enough for an operator to make progress without LLM augmentation). **Per-app SQLite cache** in new module `androscan/internal/trace_cache.py` — pure file-I/O over a `cache_dir: Path` argument with no Smali / call-graph imports so 10.6's REST routes can read directly without spinning up the full skill. Schema v1: `meta(key, value)` + `anchors(entry_smali_id, hops, payload_json, created_at, PRIMARY KEY (entry_smali_id, hops))`. Six-function API (`get_status` / `read_anchor` / `list_anchors` + `init_db` / `write_anchor` / `delete_anchor` / `clear_all_anchors`) plus the canonical `anchor_to_json(anchor) -> str` (sort_keys for diffing) + `anchor_from_json(str) -> BehaviorAnchor` decoder dispatching `PredicateOrigin` variants by `kind` discriminator. **`BehaviorAnchor` extended** in `androscan/analysis/trace_types.py` with three additive optional fields: `advanced_plans: tuple[BypassPlan, ...] = ()` (high-risk-filtered partition for 10.7's "Advanced" expander), `rationale: str = ""` (LLM-authored prose; empty when LLM call was skipped/failed), `low_confidence_decision_indices: tuple[int, ...] = ()` (instruction_index values flagged for LLM re-classification). All three default to safe empties so 10.1's minimal-shell construction pattern still works. **Two new config knobs** `trace_max_hops_default: int = 3` + `trace_max_hops_hard_cap: int = 6` wired through every loader site (`Config` dataclass + `Config.default()` + `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` + `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS` + `_merge_from_yaml` via `_safe_int` + env-override branch + `with_overrides` clamp + `coerce_yaml_value`'s `int_fields` set + `global_config.yaml` documentation). **Tests.** **+22 tests** in `tests/test_trace_behavior_skill.py`: catalog/registration (3); input validation (2); fail-open paths (4 — bare run-folder, decompile not ready, call graph not ready, unknown entry method); closure walk + per-method static layer (2 — single-method closure for `Plans.gateBoolPredicate()V` emits 1 decision + 2 plans; closure walk writes to `trace.sqlite` + visible via the pure cache reader); cache hit + force=True (2 — second call hits cache without re-invoking LLM via an unreachable stub; `force=True` re-walks even when cached and overwrites the row); LLM round-trip (3 via monkeypatched `androscan.llm.client.complete` returning a stub `_LLMResponse` — re-classification lifts confidence + appends `llm-reclassified` reason; valid LLM-proposed plan round-trips through `frida_hooks.render_by_id` + surfaces in `data.plans`; invalid template_id silently dropped); LLM fail-soft (2 — `complete()` raises → deterministic payload + `[llm-skipped: transport: ...]` marker; non-JSON response → deterministic payload + `[llm-skipped: json-parse: ...]` marker); risk partitioning (1 — `trace_bypass_risk_max="low"` config routes Plan B's MEDIUM `force_method_skip` to `advanced_plans` while Plan A's LOW `force_return_value` stays in `plans`); config-knob smoke (3 — defaults match spec, both new knobs in `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS`, both in `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP`). All 22 tests pass via the same `_seed_apps_with_graph` pattern as `test_query_call_graph_skill.py` (real apktool tree, real call-graph build, real per-method static analysis end-to-end). Test stub helpers `_LLMResponse` / `_stub_llm_unreachable` / `_stub_llm_must_not_call` / `_first_decision_index` are reusable for future trace-behavior regressions. Full suite: **727 / 727 green** (was 705; +22). No FE changes, no schema changes outside the new `trace.sqlite`, no new optional dependencies, no edits to `smali_parser.py` / `dispatch.py` / `call_graph.py`.
- notes: The choice to put the prompt builder (`build_trace_behavior_prompt`) inline in the skill module rather than `androscan/llm/prompts.py` mirrors the established pattern in `verify_exploit_result.py` (per-skill prompts inline; `prompts.py` is the workflow-agent's chat catalog) — keeps the skill self-contained so future regressions are localised + tests can pin the prompt shape via direct module import. Per-anchor (vs per-decision) LLM calls are forced by DEC-024 — the per-decision pattern would blow up the token budget on real apps with 30 methods × 5 decisions each; one LLM call with all flagged gates rendered into a structured prompt is the contract. The choice to lazy-import `androscan.llm.client.complete` inside `_invoke_llm` (rather than at module load) is what makes test stubbing via `monkeypatch.setattr("androscan.llm.client.complete", ...)` work cleanly across all 8 LLM-touching tests; module-level imports would require setattr on `tb_skill.complete` instead which is fragile to refactors. The pure-SQLite `trace_cache` module is deliberately import-free of `smali_parser` / `call_graph` so 10.6's REST routes can read directly without spinning up the full skill — the same separation that lets the Settings tab's status card render the cache state without invoking analysis. The `LLM_RECLASSIFY_CONFIDENCE = 0.75` floor is between the heuristic moderate tier (0.85) and the weak tier (0.45) deliberately — operators reading the UI can distinguish "heuristic strong (1.0)" from "LLM refined (0.75)" from "heuristic moderate (0.85)" without ambiguity, and the gap is wide enough that 10.7's UI badge logic stays simple. The `force=True` re-walk is the operator's escape hatch for when a Smali file changed (rare — usually a re-decompile changes the sha and invalidates the cache directory entirely) or when the LLM had a bad day; explicit per-anchor TTL is deferred to v2 (the sha-keyed cache directory is good enough invalidation for v1).
- follow-up: Sub-step **10.6 — REST endpoint + tab shell + Hook Lab → Lab code rename** (new `androscan/web/trace_routes.py` factory-built via `build_trace_router(get_config, get_skill_runner, run_folder_root, web_apps_root)` and wired into `androscan/web/app.py`: `GET /api/trace/{app_id}/anchor?entry=<smali_id>&hops=<n>` reads `trace.sqlite` directly without invoking the skill, `POST /api/trace/{app_id}/anchor` forces re-trace via the skill, `DELETE /api/trace/{app_id}/anchor/{anchor_id}` invalidates one cache row; `HookLabTab.tsx` renamed to `LabTab.tsx`, `#/hook` URL hash → `#/lab` with one-shot redirect, 3-mode left-rail switcher `Trace | Manual Hooks | Graph` defaulting to `Trace`; per-tab chat-prompt key in `androscan/web/chat.py` `hook_lab` → `lab` with back-compat alias). 10.6 is the first sub-step where the UI surface starts shifting — the pure-SQLite layer + the `BehaviorAnchor` JSON contract from 10.5 are the two seams 10.6's routes will read against; the rename touches `androscan/web/chat.py` + `frontend/README.md` + every frontend component file with `HookLab` in the name.

### 2026-04-29 Phase 10 (sub-step 10.4): Bypass planner + three override Frida templates
- outcome: Fourth code-bearing Phase 10 sub-step. New module `androscan/analysis/bypass_planner.py` — pure deterministic synthesiser that maps a single `(DecisionPoint, BranchOutcome)` to zero or more template-bound `BypassPlan` instances. Public surface: `plan_bypasses(decision, instructions=(), label_index=None) -> tuple[BypassPlan, ...]` (the per-decision API; 10.5's `trace_behavior` skill loops over the closure and aggregates), `partition_by_risk(plans, threshold) -> (default_plans, advanced_plans)` (operator-facing risk-threshold split — 10.7's UI surfaces `advanced_plans` behind an "Advanced" expander per DEC-024), and the locked taxonomy primitives `VALID_RISKS = ("low", "medium", "high")` + `DEFAULT_RISK_THRESHOLD = "medium"` + `risk_at_or_below(plan_risk, threshold)` (strict total-order comparator with fail-soft fallback to `"medium"` on unknown values — mirrors how the loader handles `llm_provider`). **`BypassPlan` extended** in `androscan/analysis/trace_types.py` with three additive back-reference fields per DEC-024's data-model lock: `target_method: Optional[MethodRef]` (the method actually hooked — Plan A targets the predicate's source method, Plan B targets the gate method itself, Plan C targets the synthetic `Ljava/lang/String;->equals(Ljava/lang/Object;)Z` ref since the hook is on String.equals app-wide); `source_decision_method: Optional[MethodRef]` (the gate method the plan was generated for — the back-reference 10.5's persistence layer indexes by and 10.7's `BypassPlanCard` renders alongside); `source_decision_instruction_index: Optional[int]` (which `DecisionPoint` within `source_decision_method` — methods with multiple decisions need the disambiguator). All three default to `None` so 10.1's minimal-shell construction pattern still works (the planner always populates them; tests for the partitioner construct shells without them). **Three new Frida hook templates** under `androscan/adapters/frida_hooks/` — all template-bound, no free-form JS per DEC-024, all picked up automatically by the existing `TestRegistryFailClosed` parametrized fail-closed registry walk: **`force_return_value`** (params: `class_name` / `method_name` / `return_value_expr` / `event_label`; LOW risk; hijacks one named method and returns the operator-supplied raw JS literal — the original method body is NOT executed, side effects skipped — used by Plan A); **`force_method_skip`** (params: `class_name` / `method_name` / `return_descriptor` / `event_label`; MEDIUM risk; replaces every overload with a no-op stub returning the descriptor's natural zero — `false` for `Z`, `0` for `B/S/C/I/J`, `0.0` for `F/D`, `null` for `L...;` / `[...`, nothing for `V` — the only template that handles void gates honestly since Frida refuses non-void implementations on void methods — used by Plan B); **`force_string_compare_equal`** (params: `target_literal` / `event_label`; MEDIUM risk; hooks `String.equals(Object)` AND `String.equalsIgnoreCase(String)` app-wide and force-returns `true` only when EITHER the receiver OR the argument stringifies to `target_literal` — the literal-gated nature is what makes the override survivable; a plain `force_return_value` on `String.equals` would break class loading + hash table lookups + every internal use of equals — used by Plan C). All three follow the DEC-023 sub-step 4.4 contract verbatim: declared `HookTemplateParam` schema + JS `{{` brace escaping enforced by the registry walk + non-empty `pentester_summary_template` rendered alongside the JS so the Lab UI's confirmation card shows the operator what the script will *do* in plain English + auto-registered via `_TEMPLATE_MODULES` (bumped from 6 → 9 modules; `TestRegistryFailClosed::test_module_list_is_non_empty` updated `>= 5` → `>= 9`). **Plan emission rules (locked v1, justified inline in the planner module docstring so 10.5's LLM gate + 10.7's UI can reason about them from the same source):** for each `(decision, outcome)` where `outcome.confidence > 0` AND there's both a DENY *and* an ALLOW verdict in `outcome.verdicts` (no flip target → no plans), and where `decision.is_switch` is False (switches punt to v2 — per-case forced literals are non-trivial and the operator can hand-craft via Manual Hooks for these), three independent rules each may contribute one plan in deterministic order (Plan A → Plan B → Plan C): **Plan A — flip the predicate value** (only when `predicate_origin` is `MethodCallOrigin` AND the source method is NOT `String.equals` / `equalsIgnoreCase` — those route to Plan C — AND the planner can synthesise a literal for the source method's return descriptor): emit `force_return_value` targeting the *predicate's source method* with `return_value_expr` derived from `(decision.kind, allow-branch direction, source method's return descriptor)` via the locked `_SINGLE_REG_TRUE_TAKES_ZERO` table for `if-*z` opcodes and the `_zero_for_descriptor` / `_nonzero_for_descriptor` helpers — `Z → "true" / "false"`, `I/J/B/S/C → "1" / "0"`, `F/D → "1.0" / "0.0"`, `L...;` zero side → `"null"`, `L...;` non-null side → no plan (can't reliably synthesise an instance — operator authors via Manual Hooks), `V` → no plan (can't force-return on a void source method); risk LOW (precise — one named method, one literal). Two-register predicates (`if-eq` / `if-ne` / etc.) and switches are filtered upstream → no Plan A (the slicer's priority rule returns one operand; the other is unknown to the planner). **Plan B — force-skip the gate method** (only when `decision.method.return_descriptor == "V"`): emit `force_method_skip` targeting the enclosing gate method with `return_descriptor="V"`; risk MEDIUM (skips legitimate side effects too — operator must verify gate semantics before injecting). Useful for `void enforceLicense() throws SecurityException` style gates that throw on deny — skipping the method short-circuits the throw and lets execution continue past the call site. **Plan C — string-literal bypass** (only when `predicate_origin` is `MethodCallOrigin` AND source method is `String.equals` / `equalsIgnoreCase` AND a recoverable `const-string` literal exists in either branch's basic block OR via a backward scan over the gate method body up to the decision — gates frequently load the secret right above the equals call, e.g. `const-string v0, "LICENSE_VALID_42"` then `invoke-virtual {p1, v0}, String.equals`): emit `force_string_compare_equal` with `target_literal` filled from the const-string text verbatim; risk MEDIUM (app-wide `String.equals` interception, but only fires when the literal appears in either side — blast-radius limited to other gates comparing against the exact same literal, rare for non-trivial tokens). **New config knob** `trace_bypass_risk_max` (string, default `"medium"`) wired through every loader site in `androscan/config/loader.py`: `Config` dataclass + `Config.default()` + `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP[trace_bypass_risk_max] = ("trace", "bypass_risk_max", "ANDROSCAN_TRACE_BYPASS_RISK_MAX")` + `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS` (read per-call by 10.5's skill, no in-flight state to bounce) + `_merge_from_yaml` (parses new `trace:` section, fail-soft to `"medium"` on missing / empty / invalid) + env-override branch + `with_overrides` clamp + `coerce_yaml_value`'s `str_fields` set. `global_config.yaml` gets a new `trace:` section documenting the default + the valid enum + the fail-soft policy. Loader stays permissive (any string accepted at config-load time); planner / 10.5 skill validate against `{"low", "medium", "high"}` downstream with fail-soft fallback to `"medium"` (mirrors how `llm_provider` is handled — strings out-of-set don't blow up config loading; consumers handle the fallback). **Tests.** **+29 tests** in `tests/test_bypass_planner.py` (run end-to-end through the static layer `parse_classes → parse_decisions → slice_predicate_origins → classify_branch_outcomes → plan_bypasses` so each test is anchored to real Smali rather than synthesised data — catching contract drifts between layers that pure-unit tests would miss): eight Plan A tests (bool predicate emits force_return_true; int predicate emits force_return_one; ref-zero side emits force_return_null; ref-non-null side honestly skips Plan A; ConstOrigin / FieldReadOrigin / ParamOrigin / CompositeOrigin all skip Plan A; the tests use the existing `denyAllowSplit(Z)V` from 10.3's `Outcomes.smali` for the ParamOrigin coverage to demonstrate fixture reuse across phase sub-steps); two Plan B tests (void gate emits force_method_skip with `target_method == source_decision_method`; non-void gate skips Plan B but still emits Plan A); three Plan C tests (literal-found via backward scan emits force_string_compare_equal with `target_literal="LICENSE_VALID_42"`; literal-missing skips Plan C; String.equals routes away from Plan A so we don't double-emit a catastrophic blanket force-true); five planner-skip tests (no `branch_outcome` → empty; `confidence == 0.0` → empty via 10.3's `neutralWhenSymmetric`; both-deny → empty; switch via 10.3's `switchOutcomes` → empty; no-instructions arg skips Plan C honestly); seven risk-taxonomy tests (locked enum order; default threshold; total-order comparator inclusive; fail-soft on unknown; threshold-medium splits low+medium-default vs high-advanced; threshold-low pushes medium to advanced; threshold-high keeps everything default); one end-to-end integration test that the bool fixture's Plan A + Plan B output renders cleanly through `frida_hooks.render_by_id` (closes the loop on "planner output is consumable by the renderer without further massaging"); three config-knob tests (default value; live-reloadable membership; field map shape). **+3 new per-template smoke renders** in `tests/test_frida_hook_templates.py::TestTemplates` (each new template gets substring assertions on JS body + summary + sensitive-APIs metadata, mirroring the existing `entry_exit_log` / `scope_inspector` test shape). **+21 free** from the parametrized `TestRegistryFailClosed` walk auto-picking up the three new templates (7 walks × 3 modules = 21 new parametrized invocations covering the structural invariants — module exports TEMPLATE, id matches module basename, JS non-empty, summary non-empty, JS placeholders subset of declared params, summary placeholders subset, every required param appears in JS or summary, template renders with placeholder inputs). New fixture `tests/fixtures/trace_smali/smali/com/trace/Plans.smali` — 12 methods + constructor, every method shaped so the pipeline `parse → slice → classify → plan_bypasses` emits a fully-deterministic plan list (the general pattern: predicate value sourced via a recognisable means; false branch carries an ALLOW signal via `setResult` so the classifier emits a clean DENY/ALLOW pair; true branch carries a strong DENY signal via `throw` — except where the test specifically wants both-deny or symmetric-neutral). Full suite: **705 / 705 green** (was 649; +56 — 29 planner tests + 3 frida smoke renders + 21 free parametrized walks + 3 fixture-pickup deltas in earlier registry-walk parametrized tests). No FE changes, no schema changes, no new optional dependencies. No edits to `smali_parser.py` / `dispatch.py` / `call_graph.py` or any SQLite schema.
- notes: The choice to make Plan A's "non-null reference" direction honestly skip rather than emit a placeholder plan with `return_value_expr="null"` (which would take the *zero* side, wrong direction) preserves the planner's "if I emit a plan, it works" contract — operators who see a Plan A in the UI can trust it will flip the gate. The alternative (emit a `null` plan with a "WARNING: this returns null, you probably want a non-null instance" rationale) was rejected because it forces every operator to read the rationale carefully on every plan, defeating the deterministic-vs-LLM split. The MethodCallOrigin → Plan A path requires the source method to NOT be String.equals because a blanket `force_return_value` on `java.lang.String.equals` would break class loading + hash table lookups + every internal use of equals catastrophically; routing String.equals to Plan C (which is literal-gated) keeps the override survivable. The decision to skip switches entirely for v1 is conservative — switches *can* be bypassed via per-case forced literals on the predicate's source method, but the value-to-literal mapping per-case is non-trivial (you need to know which case label the operator wants to take, which is more nuanced than the if-* binary direction), and 10.5's LLM can recommend a hand-rolled hook for these. The decision to populate `target_method` / `source_decision_method` / `source_decision_instruction_index` as additive optional fields on `BypassPlan` (rather than bake them into a new `BypassPlanRef` wrapper) keeps 10.1's minimal-shell construction pattern working — tests for the partitioner construct empty-shell `BypassPlan(template_id="x", params={}, rationale="", risk="low")` instances without populating the back-references, and the existing minimal-shell pattern in `BehaviorAnchor.plans` doesn't need to change. The risk taxonomy is a closed three-value enum (`low / medium / high`) rather than a continuous numeric scale because operators reason about risk in discrete buckets and the UI renders threshold pickers as 3 radio buttons; v2 may add a `critical` tier if operators ask for it. The fail-soft on unknown risk values (coerce to `medium` on both sides of the comparator) prevents a typo in `global_config.yaml` from silently dropping all plans below `low` or surfacing all plans above `high`. The Plan A return-literal synthesis logic is the most consequential v1 design call — operators may want a *specific* non-zero value (e.g. a magic `0xDEADBEEF` int) rather than the generic `1`, but v1 picks the simplest distinguishable literal and lets the operator hand-edit before injecting (the `HookBuilder` UI in 10.7 will surface the rendered JS in Monaco for hand-editing, same flow as the Manual Hooks mode). The `force_string_compare_equal` template's literal-gated semantics (only fires when the literal appears in either side) is intentionally narrower than the spec's "force string equals to true" interpretation — the unguarded version was prototyped during 10.4 design and immediately failed to attach because the JVM's class loader threw on the first internal `String.equals` call.
- follow-up: Sub-step **10.5 — `trace_behavior` LLM-tier skill** (new `androscan/skills/trace_behavior.py`: read-only LLM-tier skill that walks the call-graph forward closure ≤ `MAX_TRACE_HOPS` and ≤ `MAX_TRACE_METHODS = 30` from a given entry method, runs `decisions.parse_decisions → slicing.slice_predicate_origins → branch_classifier.classify_branch_outcomes → bypass_planner.plan_bypasses` over each method in the closure, builds a populated `BehaviorAnchor` payload, and feeds it to the LLM **once per anchor** for re-classification of low-confidence gates + rationale authoring + bypass plan proposals for cases the deterministic planner returned empty; persists the populated anchor to `apps/<app_id>/.decompiled/<sha>/trace.sqlite`). 10.5 is the first sub-step that wires the per-decision static layer into the per-anchor LLM layer — the four enrichments from 10.1–10.4 (decision + slice + classification + plan) all flow into one LLM call per anchor.

### 2026-04-29 Phase 10 (sub-step 10.3): Heuristic branch outcome classifier
- outcome: Third code-bearing Phase 10 sub-step. New module `androscan/analysis/branch_classifier.py` — pure deterministic per-branch deny/allow/neutral classifier. Public API: `classify_branch_outcomes(method_decisions) -> MethodDecisions` (frozen-dataclass-replace, no input mutation; mirrors 10.2's slicer API exactly so 10.5's `trace_behavior` skill can chain `parse_decisions → slice_predicate_origins → classify_branch_outcomes` without intermediate bookkeeping); `classify_one_decision(decision, instructions, label_index)` for tests / 10.5's per-anchor walk where the caller may hold the decision separately. Two new dataclasses added to `androscan/analysis/trace_types.py`: `BranchVerdict(branch_label, verdict, score, reasons)` (one per `Branch` in the parent `DecisionPoint.branches`, same order; `branch_label` matches the corresponding `Branch.label` so consumers can join by index OR by label) and `BranchOutcome(verdicts, confidence, reasons)`. `DecisionPoint` extended with `branch_outcome: Optional[BranchOutcome] = None` (additive default → all 29 of 10.1's + 10.2's tests still pass unchanged; verified before any 10.3 test was authored). **Heuristic catalog (locked v1, justified inline in the module docstring so 10.4 / 10.5 / 10.7 can reason about confidence tiers from the same source):** strong DENY (-1.0): `throw vN` (exception unconditionally raised), `Ljava/lang/System;->exit` (process termination), `Landroid/os/Process;->killProcess` (process killed by PID). Strong ALLOW (+1.0): `L...;->setResult(...)` (Activity reports a result back; v1 doesn't distinguish RESULT_OK vs RESULT_CANCELED — both signal "this branch reports a result"; 10.5's LLM can refine via the const slice that precedes the invoke). Moderate DENY (-0.7): `L...;->finish\|finishAffinity\|finishAndRemoveTask()V` *only when no preceding `setResult` in the same basic block* (the legitimate "I reported a result and now I'm closing" allow-flow is suppressed via order-aware walk — `setresult_seen` flag tracked through the linear scan); `const-string vN, "..."` whose literal matches the curated deny-keyword regex. Moderate ALLOW (+0.7): `L...;->startActivity(ForResult)?(...)` (a new Activity is launched — the happy path continues). Weak DENY (-0.3): branch-length ratio ≥ 3:1 with shorter branch ≤ 3 instructions, applied *only* when both branches are otherwise neutral (the early-exit-gate pattern — short deny-branch + long real-work-branch — when no explicit signal fired). Restricted to 2-branch ifs only; switches use per-case verdicts directly without cross-branch ratio. **Verdict thresholds:** `score ≤ -0.5 → "deny"`, `score ≥ +0.5 → "allow"`, else `"neutral"`. **Confidence tiers (deterministic, pinned by tests so 10.5 / 10.7 can rely on them as a stable contract):** based on `max(|branch.score|)` across all branches — `≥ 1.0 → 1.00` (strong signal — operator can trust without LLM review), `≥ 0.7 → 0.85` (moderate / string-keyword signal — still above the 0.6 threshold, no LLM re-classification needed), `≥ 0.3 → 0.45` (weak signal only — *below* the `LLM_RECLASSIFY_THRESHOLD = 0.6` constant exported from the module; 10.5's `trace_behavior` skill will invoke the LLM to re-classify), else `0.00` (no signals — LLM re-classifies). Pre-classification, `branch_outcome=None`; post-classification it's *always* populated (even when verdicts are all neutral and confidence is 0.0) so 10.5 distinguishes "didn't run the classifier" (`is None`) from "ran it and found nothing" (`confidence == 0.0`). **Curated deny-keyword regex** extends DEC-024's spec `\b(premium|locked|jailbroken|unauthori[sz]ed|forbidden|denied|tamper|root|debug)\b` with two strict-additive improvements documented in the module docstring: (1) word boundaries (`\b...\b`) around the alternation prevent partial-word false positives — `rootView` does not match `root`, `debugger` does not match `debug` (matched via the explicit `(?:ger|ging)?` suffix instead); (2) optional `(?:ed|ing)?` / `(?:ger|ging)?` suffixes on `tamper` / `root` / `debug` so canonical pentest vocabulary like `rooted` / `tampered` matches (without these, the spec would miss "Device is rooted" — the textbook root-detection string). Both extensions are operator-facing improvements that strictly enlarge the match set; v2 may add more pentest vocabulary (`frida`, `xposed`, `emulator`, etc.) per operator feedback. **Basic-block walker** (`_basic_block`) walks forward from a branch target's first instruction (or `decision_instruction_index + 1` for fall-through / switch default) until hitting a basic-block terminator (`return*` / `throw` / `goto*` / `if-*` / `*-switch`); the terminator IS part of the returned block since `throw` is itself a deny signal. End-of-method-body acts as an implicit terminator. Unresolved labels return an empty block → 0-score → neutral verdict (defensive; well-formed apktool output never hits this). **Tests.** **+19 tests** (slightly over the planned ~18 budget) in `tests/test_branch_classifier.py`: pre-classify `branch_outcome` defaults to `None` on 10.2's output (baseline contract); `classify_branch_outcomes` returns a fresh `MethodDecisions` and doesn't mutate input (frozen-replace contract); per-signal assertions for `throw` / `System.exit` / `killProcess` (strong DENY, score -1.0, confidence 1.0) + `setResult` (strong ALLOW, +1.0, conf 1.0) + `startActivity` (moderate ALLOW, +0.7, conf 0.85); `finish` without `setResult` yields moderate DENY (-0.7, confidence 0.85, reason text contains "finish without setResult"); `setResult` preceding `finish` suppresses the finish signal (net score +1.0 — pure ALLOW; the verdict's `reasons` list explicitly does NOT contain "finish without setResult"); string-keyword matches for `rooted` (suffix-aware regex extension), `PREMIUM` (case-insensitive), `unauthorised` (British `s` not `z` — verifies the `[sz]` alternation); `rootView focused` is NOT matched by `\broot(?:ed|ing)?\b` (word-boundary contract — verdict NEUTRAL, score 0.0, no signal in reasons); length-ratio fixture (1-instruction true branch vs 8-instruction false branch, ratio 8:1) yields weak DENY (-0.3) on shorter side, NEUTRAL on longer side, confidence 0.45 explicitly *below* `LLM_RECLASSIFY_THRESHOLD` (asserted via the exported constant), method-level reason surfaces the cross-branch ratio; symmetric-no-signals method → both verdicts NEUTRAL/0.0/empty reasons + confidence 0.0; packed-switch with throw/setResult/neutral cases + neutral default classifies each case independently (case 0 DENY -1.0, case 1 ALLOW +1.0, case 2 + default NEUTRAL 0.0) with confidence 1.0 from case 0's strong throw signal; clean two-branch DENY/ALLOW split (throw on one side, setResult on the other) — both verdicts non-neutral, both scores ±1.0, confidence 1.0; **confidence-tier pinning test** asserts the four documented tier values (1.00 / 0.85 / 0.45 / 0.00) one-per-fixture so 10.5 / 10.7 can rely on them as a stable wire contract; `BranchOutcome` JSON round-trip via `dataclasses.asdict` + `json.dumps` (the contract 10.5's `trace.sqlite` cache + 10.6's wire format both depend on). New fixture `tests/fixtures/trace_smali/smali/com/trace/Outcomes.smali` (15 methods + constructor; each method exercises exactly one heuristic with a hand-crafted basic-block layout so test assertions are fully deterministic — predictable per-branch verdict + score + confidence). One pre-existing 10.1 test loosened (`summary.switches == 2 → >= 2`) since the new `Outcomes.smali` introduces a third packed-switch — original intent was "at least the packed + sparse from SwitchCases", lower bound keeps the assertion robust against fixture growth without losing coverage of the switch-counting path itself. Full suite: **649 / 649 green** (was 630; +19). No FE changes, no schema changes, no new optional dependencies.
- notes: The choice to populate `branch_outcome` for ALL post-classify decisions (even when all verdicts are neutral and confidence is 0.0) over leaving it `None` preserves the "did we run the classifier?" / "what did the classifier say?" distinction explicitly — 10.5 needs to tell "this gate hasn't been classified yet" apart from "this gate was classified and the heuristics found nothing actionable" because the second case still triggers LLM re-classification (different cache/work logic). The `setResult`-suppresses-`finish` rule is the most opinionated v1 choice; the alternative (additive scoring → setResult +1.0 cancels finish -0.7 = +0.3 → NEUTRAL by threshold) loses the operator's mental model of "this branch is reporting success cleanly". Order-aware suppression preserves that signal: if `finish` precedes `setResult` in the block (unusual but legal), the finish-deny signal still fires — the rule only suppresses the legitimate "report-then-close" pattern. The deny-keyword regex extension is justified inline in the module docstring; without the suffix variants, common pentest detection strings like "Device is rooted" / "App was tampered" would slip past the heuristic, defeating the whole point of the keyword check. The 4 confidence tiers are deliberately discrete (not continuous) — 10.5's LLM gate is a hard `< 0.6` threshold, and the UI in 10.7 will surface tier names ("strong" / "moderate" / "weak" / "none") rather than raw floats, so discrete values give the operator a stable mental model. Length-ratio thresholds (≥ 3:1 with shorter ≤ 3) are conservative — tighter than this generates noise in normal code; looser misses the canonical early-exit-gate pattern. Tests pin the boundary (1-instruction vs 8-instruction = 8:1 ratio; would also fire at 3-instruction vs 9-instruction = 3:1). v2 may make these per-app configurable via `trace.length_ratio_*` but v1 holds them as code constants for predictable behaviour across apps.
- follow-up: Sub-step **10.4 — Bypass planner** (new `androscan/analysis/bypass_planner.py`: maps `(DecisionPoint, BranchOutcome)` → zero or more `BypassPlan` instances each referencing one of `frida_hooks/`'s templates). 10.4 is the first sub-step where the static layer's three enrichments (decision + slice + classification) all pay off together — the planner needs all three to choose template + parameters: `branch_outcome.verdicts` to identify which branch is "deny" vs "allow"; `predicate_origin` (10.2) to pick the right hook template (`MethodCallOrigin` → `force_return_value`, `FieldReadOrigin` → `patch_field`, `ConstOrigin` → no plan since you can't patch a const without re-decompiling, `ParamOrigin` → `intercept_arg`, `CompositeOrigin` → no plan, too ambiguous for v1); `decision.method` (10.1) for the hook target. Three new templates ship with the same DEC-023 sub-step 4.4 contract (declared params + JS `{{` brace escaping + non-empty `pentester_summary_template` + the fail-closed `TestRegistryFailClosed` walk extends to them automatically). New tests in `tests/test_bypass_planner.py` (~20 tests — per-template plan emission + risk-threshold filtering + rationale text shape + new templates render under the existing registry-walk invariants).

### 2026-04-28 Phase 10 (sub-step 10.2): Backward slicing for predicate origin
- outcome: Second code-bearing Phase 10 sub-step. New module `androscan/analysis/slicing.py` — pure intra-procedural backward slicer that walks each `DecisionPoint`'s `predicate_registers` backward through the method body to its closest defining instruction. Public surface: `slice_predicate_origins(method_decisions, *, max_walk=64) -> MethodDecisions` (frozen-dataclass-replace, no input mutation) plus `slice_one_decision(decision, instructions)` for 10.5's per-anchor walk where the caller may hold the decision separately from the enclosing `MethodDecisions`. Five `*Origin` dataclasses added to `androscan/analysis/trace_types.py` per DEC-024: `MethodCallOrigin(method, invoke_kind)` for `invoke-* + move-result-*` chains; `FieldReadOrigin(field, is_static)` for `iget-*` (instance) and `sget-*` (static); `ConstOrigin(value, smali_op)` for `const-*` literal loads with the raw text preserved verbatim (`"premium"` not `premium`); `ParamOrigin(register)` for unmodified Smali parameter registers (walk reached start-of-method with a `pN` tracked); `CompositeOrigin(reason)` for arithmetic / `instance-of` / `cmp-long` / `array-length` / `new-instance` / casts / `move-exception` / etc. — v1 deliberately doesn't break composites down further per DEC-024's intra-procedural slicing limitation. Each origin variant carries a `kind` discriminator field (`"method_call"` / `"field_read"` / `"const"` / `"param"` / `"composite"`) so `dataclasses.asdict` + `json.dumps` round-trips the union variant unambiguously — 10.5's `trace.sqlite` cache + 10.6's wire format both deserialise via a `kind`-keyed dispatch with no custom encoder needed. `DecisionPoint` extended with `predicate_origin: Optional[PredicateOrigin] = None` (additive default → all 13 of 10.1's tests still pass unchanged; verified before any 10.2 test was authored). Slicer behaviour: follows `move(-wide|-object)?(/from16|/16)?` aliases transparently (replaces tracked register with source register and keeps walking — so `iget v1 → move v0, v1 → if-eqz v0` resolves to the underlying FieldReadOrigin, not a "move" composite); `move-result-*` looks back one step for the preceding `invoke-*` and emits MethodCallOrigin from its target signature (orphan `move-result-*` falls back to CompositeOrigin); skip-set excludes opcodes that don't define registers (`iput-*` / `sput-*` / `aput-*` / `invoke-*` / `goto*` / `return*` / `throw` / `monitor-*` / `check-cast` / `nop` / `fill-array-data` / `filled-new-array*`); walk exhaustion via `max_walk` (default 64) → `predicate_origin = None` honestly per DEC-024 rather than fabricating an origin (10.5 will then either flag the gate as low-confidence or invoke the LLM with the raw method body); walk reaches start-of-method with a `pN` tracked → ParamOrigin, with a `vN` tracked (uninitialised — defensive) → None. **Two-register predicate combination policy (DEC-024 v1):** for `if-eq` / `if-ne` / `if-lt` / `if-le` / `if-gt` / `if-ge`, the slicer slices each operand independently then combines via the priority rule `MethodCall > FieldRead > Param > Const > Composite > None`; the more-actionable side wins (operators typically want to manipulate the non-constant operand of value comparisons — a comparison against a constant encodes "compare against this value", and the value itself is rarely the bypass lever). Ties break to the LHS so source order produces a stable result. v2 may widen `predicate_origin` to a `tuple[PredicateOrigin, ...]` when both sides matter equally. **Small amendment to `decisions.py`:** `MethodDecisions` gained an additive `instructions: tuple[str, ...] = ()` field carrying the raw stripped text of every "real" instruction line indexed by `instruction_index`; populated via a tiny `_emit_instruction(raw)` helper in `_MethodState` that's now the single source of truth for both the index counter and the instructions list (every previous `self._instruction_index += 1` site was migrated). Memory cost: only methods with at least one branch retain their bodies — typical APK is a few hundred KB. This is what enables the slicer to be a pure function over `MethodDecisions` without re-reading files. **Tests.** **+16 tests** in `tests/test_decisions_slicing.py`: `predicate_origin` defaults to None on 10.1 output (pre-slice baseline contract); `instructions` is populated and points at `if-` / `*-switch` lines at the indices each `DecisionPoint` records; per-variant slicer assertions for MethodCallOrigin (invoke-static + move-result + if-eqz; `invoke_kind` and `MethodRef.smali_signature` round-trip), FieldReadOrigin (iget — instance) + FieldReadOrigin (sget — static) covered separately, ConstOrigin (`const/4 v0, 0x1`) + ConstOrigin (`const-string v0, "premium"` — string literal preserved verbatim including quotes), ParamOrigin (`if-eqz p1` with no preceding def — register `"p1"`), CompositeOrigin (arithmetic — `add-int`), CompositeOrigin (`instance-of`), CompositeOrigin (`move-exception` from a try/catch fixture); move chain `iget v1 → move v0, v1 → if-eqz v0` resolves to the underlying FieldReadOrigin (slicer follows aliases); two-register `iget vs const` returns FieldReadOrigin via the priority rule; two-register both-MethodCall returns the LHS via tie-breaking; `max_walk=2` exhaustion against a method with the def 12 instructions back returns None (and the same method with `max_walk=64` resolves to ConstOrigin — both assertions in one test); JSON round-trip across all 5 variants verifies the `kind` discriminator survives `dataclasses.asdict` + `json.dumps`. New fixture `tests/fixtures/trace_smali/smali/com/trace/Slices.smali` — ~13 methods + 4 helpers, hand-crafted so every origin kind has a known terminus and the test assertions can be fully deterministic (each method exercises exactly one slicer code path). Full suite: **630 / 630 green** (was 614; +16). No FE changes, no schema changes, no new optional dependencies.
- notes: The choice to put `instructions` on `MethodDecisions` rather than re-parse the file in the slicer is justified by the slicer being a pure function — it doesn't carry file-path state and never needs to know where the smali came from. Memory cost for a typical APK (a few hundred KB across all methods with branches) is tiny vs. the ergonomic + testability win of pure-function semantics. The two-register priority rule is the most opinionated v1 choice; the alternatives considered were (a) returning a tuple of origins (richer but changes the data-model contract before 10.3/10.4 wire it up), (b) returning Composite for any two-register comparison (loses information), (c) returning the LHS unconditionally (less informative when LHS is Const). The chosen rule preserves "one PredicateOrigin per DecisionPoint" while giving 10.4's bypass planner a usable lever in 90% of realistic gates. Two-non-Const cases still return *one* origin but the operator can drill into the source for the other operand if needed. The slicer's defaults (`max_walk=64`) are deliberately generous — most realistic gates resolve in <10 walked instructions. The walk cap exists to bound 10.5's per-anchor budget, not to filter out long-chain definitions.
- follow-up: Sub-step **10.3 — Heuristic branch outcome classifier** (new `androscan/analysis/branch_classifier.py`: deterministic per-branch classification into `deny` / `allow` / `neutral` based on basic-block content — System.exit / throw / Activity.finish detection, branch-length heuristics, string-constant regex scoring, weak-signal `setResult(RESULT_OK)` / `startActivity` patterns; emits `BranchOutcome(true_branch, false_branch, confidence, reasons)` per DecisionPoint; gates with `confidence < 0.6` flagged for LLM re-classification in 10.5). 10.3 is the first sub-step where the deterministic-vs-LLM split actually shows up — heuristics produce the confident classifications, LLM only sees the ambiguous ones.

### 2026-04-28 Phase 10 (sub-step 10.1): Decision-point extraction + platform-neutral data model
- outcome: First code-bearing Phase 10 sub-step. Two new modules under `androscan/analysis/`. **`trace_types.py`** ships the platform-neutral data model per DEC-024: `MethodRef` and `FieldRef` (Java-form names + raw Smali descriptors with `smali_signature` round-trip joining `call_graph.nodes`); `DecisionKind` (string-valued enum covering all 12 if-* opcodes + packed/sparse switch; `is_two_register_predicate` / `is_switch` accessors); `Branch(label, target_label: Optional[str])` (`None` = fall-through); `DecisionPoint` (the deliverable of 10.1 — `method` / `instruction_index` / `source_line` / `kind` / `predicate_registers` / `branches`); `BehaviorAnchor` and `BypassPlan` shipped as minimal shells locking field names 10.4 / 10.5 will populate without breaking the contract. **`decisions.py`** is a third Smali pass over the same apktool tree `smali_parser.py` already walks twice — `parse_classes` and `parse_invokes` are completely untouched. `parse_decisions(roots, classes) -> (list[MethodDecisions], DecisionsParseSummary)` emits one `MethodDecisions` per method with at least one branch (linear methods are excluded — callers that need full enumeration join against the existing `ClassDecl.methods` list). Switch data blocks (`:pswitch_data_0` + `.packed-switch <start>` / `:sswitch_data_0` + `.sparse-switch`) are parsed inline via a deferred-back-fill pattern (`_pending_switches` dict in `_MethodState`) so each switch decision's `branches` are populated with one `Branch` per case label + a `("default", None)` fall-through; the per-method `label_index: tuple[(label_name, instruction_index), ...]` lets 10.3's basic-block walker resolve any branch target without re-parsing the file. Instruction indexing is conservative — only lowercase-opcode lines advance the index; `.directives`, `:labels`, `# comments`, blank lines do not — so labels point at real next-instruction positions. Fail-soft on malformed switch blocks: `_DecisionParseError` caught at file level, surfaced via `DecisionsParseSummary.parse_errors`, rest of file keeps parsing (mirrors `smali_parser`'s posture). **No edits to `smali_parser.py`, `dispatch.py`, `call_graph.py`, or any SQLite schema** — additive only. **Tests.** New `tests/test_decisions_extract.py` (**13 tests**): MethodRef + FieldRef round-trip (including malformed-input `ValueError`), linear method excluded from output, summary counters match emitted decisions, `if-eqz` 2-branch shape with correct register + true-target + fall-through (modelled as `target_label=None`), all six zero-comparison kinds covered in one method, all six two-register kinds covered with predicate-register tuples, source-line attachment tracks most recent `.line` directive, label_index resolves branch targets to next-instruction indices, packed-switch emits one branch per case + default, sparse-switch preserves original key strings (`"case 0xa"` not `"case 10"`), multi-dex coverage via `smali_classes2/HiddenGate.smali`, and `DecisionPoint` round-trips through `dataclasses.asdict` + `json.dumps` (the contract 10.5's `trace.sqlite` cache + 10.6's wire format both depend on). Three new fixture .smali files under `tests/fixtures/trace_smali/` (`smali/com/trace/Gates.smali` exercising every if-* opcode + a realistic `checkRoot` / `openPremium` gate; `smali/com/trace/SwitchCases.smali` with packed + sparse switch coverage; `smali_classes2/com/trace/HiddenGate.smali` for multi-dex). Full suite: **614 / 614 green** (was 601; +13). No FE changes, no schema changes, no new optional dependencies.
- notes: The branch model uses `target_label: Optional[str]` where `None` means fall-through, rather than synthesising an explicit fall-through label. This is honest about the Smali model (fall-through is implicit — the next instruction wins) and lets the 10.3 basic-block walker join `target_label` against the `label_index` without a special-case for synthetic labels. The `MethodDecisions` aggregate excludes linear methods (no branches) entirely — keeping the result list tight for downstream consumers; the trade-off is that 10.5 has to join against the full method list when it walks a closure, but that join is already needed for the call-graph forward closure walk so no extra cost. `BehaviorAnchor` and `BypassPlan` shipped as minimal-but-locked shells (canonical field names only) per DEC-024's "additive-fields-OK" rule — adding new optional fields to a frozen dataclass after consumers are wired is harder than locking the canonical names now.
- follow-up: Sub-step **10.2 — Backward slicing for predicate origin** (new `androscan/analysis/slicing.py`: intra-procedural backward slice from each `DecisionPoint`'s `predicate_register` to its closest defining instruction; emits `predicate_origin: Optional[PredicateOrigin]` discriminated union of `MethodCall` / `FieldRead` / `Const` / `Param` / `Composite` / `None`). 10.2 is the consumer that proves out the `MethodRef` / `FieldRef` shape from 10.1 — if the round-trip needs a tweak it surfaces there before 10.3 starts depending on it.

### 2026-04-28 Phase 10 (sub-step 10.0): Planning checkpoint + DEC-024 + Hook Lab → Lab rename pin
- outcome: Docs-only planning checkpoint that locks Phase 10 — Behavior Trace v1 before any code lands. **DEC-024** added to `docs/DECISIONS.md` covering the workflow framing (client-side trust manipulation: *Anchor → Locate → Trace → Classify → Manipulate → Verify* — explicitly **not** source/sink reachability), the platform-neutral data model (`BehaviorAnchor` / `DecisionPoint` / `BypassPlan` / `MethodRef` / `FieldRef` ship in 10.1; Android is one adapter, iOS / desktop / embedded land later behind the same contract), the static-enumerate / LLM-interpret split (one LLM call per anchor — per-decision explicitly rejected), the template-bound bypass planner (no free-form LLM JS in v1; `generate_frida_hook` keeps that operator-driven path separate), the closure caps (`MAX_TRACE_HOPS = 3` default / hard cap 6 / `MAX_TRACE_METHODS = 30`), the intra-procedural slicing limitation (honestly surfaced via `predicate_origin: None` + a "trace may be incomplete" banner), the Cytoscape demotion (kept as Lab's `Graph` mode alongside `Trace` and `Manual Hooks`; demoted, not deleted), per-app SQLite at `apps/<app_id>/.decompiled/<sha>/trace.sqlite` (schema_version 1, mirrors the DEC-016 / DEC-018 / DEC-023 `<sha>`-keyed cache pattern), the `BypassPlan` risk taxonomy (`low / medium / high` + operator-configurable `trace.bypass_risk_max` threshold defaulting `medium`; high-risk plans hidden behind an "Advanced" expander), deterministic test discipline (LLM mocked at the boundary, fixture-driven static layer, ~80-test target), and the **Hook Lab → Lab** rename policy (forward-looking only; historical references in DEC-023 / Hook Lab v1 backlog / completed-task entries / ISSUE-010 / 011 / 012 / LIMIT-002 retain "Hook Lab" as accurate proper nouns; code rename `HookLabTab.tsx` → `LabTab.tsx` etc. deferred to sub-step **10.6** alongside tab routing changes). Cross-link bullets added: **DEC-016** related-docs gains DEC-024 forward pointer (Phase 10 reuses the call-graph SQLite store unchanged); **DEC-023** related-docs gains DEC-024 forward pointer (Phase 10 reuses every Hook Lab v1 substrate; rename is concurrent with this DEC). **`docs/TASKS.md` updates:** Active Task line points at Phase 10; Phase 8 / Phase 9 status rows stale-fixed (both shipped via Hook Lab v1 — were still flagged "Not started"); new Phase 10 status row added; **§ Phase 10 — Behavior Trace v1 — sub-step backlog** table inserted (10.0 → 10.8 with deliverable + dependency columns, 10.0 marked `[x]` in the same commit per DEC-024); brief Phase 10 — Behavior Trace section added at the same nesting as Phase 6 / 7 / 8 / 9 historical sub-sections; **Naming note** added under the § Interactive RE Workbench heading flagging the rename policy. **`docs/DESIGN_DOC.md` updates:** new Phase 10 stub added after Phase 9 with goal + workflow + deliverable summary + cross-link to DEC-024 and the Phase 10 sub-step backlog. **No code changes, no test changes, no frontend changes** — `git diff` shows only `.md` files.
- notes: This sub-step is the planning checkpoint itself — DEC-024 + the docs scaffolding it lights up. The intent (per DEC-024 follow-up) is that 10.1 → 10.8 then ship strictly linearly, one sub-step per Agent-mode session, with a brief Ask-mode planning checkpoint at the top of 10.6 to confirm the `BehaviorAnchor` JSON wire shape before the frontend in 10.7 starts depending on it (mirrors the 4.1 SQLite-schema checkpoint that locked the call-graph store before the routes API). The Hook Lab → Lab rename is intentionally docs-policy-only at 10.0; the actual code rename (`HookLabTab.tsx` → `LabTab.tsx`, `HookLabCodeView` → `LabCodeView`, `#/hook` URL hash → `#/lab`, the per-tab chat-prompt key in `androscan/web/chat.py`, `frontend/README.md`) lands in 10.6 alongside the tab routing changes — keeping Phase 10's docs-vs-code commits cleanly separated. Until then, code-level identifiers in DEC-024 and the Phase 10 sub-step entries reference the existing `HookLabTab.tsx` filename for accuracy. The Hook Lab v1 historical text across `docs/STATE.md`, `docs/TASKS.md` (Hook Lab v1 backlog table), `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` (ISSUE-010 / 011 / 012, LIMIT-002), and `docs/SAFETY_AND_SECURITY.md` §12.6 is **not** rewritten — those entries describe completed work under the name it shipped under, which is the right historical record.
- follow-up: Sub-step **10.1 — Decision-point extraction** (new `androscan/analysis/decisions.py` extending `smali_parser.py` with one extra pass over the instruction stream emitting `DecisionPoint`s per Smali conditional branch + `androscan/analysis/trace_types.py` for the platform-neutral data model). ### 2026-04 Phase 6 polish (UX step 3.5): Settings tab — global + per-app + status + diagnostics
- outcome: New top-level `Settings` tab (`#/settings`) covering global config, per-app overrides, live status, and diagnostics. Backend: `androscan/web/health_probes.py` (timeboxed pure-function async probes for adb/jadx/apktool/frida/Ollama/embed-provider/disk + per-app pm-path/foreground/UID/uiautomator/apk-sha-drift/RAG/decompile-cache; never raise; `{ok, label, ...}` shape); `androscan/web/per_app_settings.py` (atomic schema-versioned writes to `apps/<app_id>/app_settings.json`, `effective_settings(global_view, per_app)` merger, `apk_overrides_summary`); `androscan/web/status_routes.py` (`GET /api/status/global`, `GET /api/status/apps/{app_id}` — `asyncio.gather` + 3 s in-process cache; `invalidate_status_cache()` on every settings save/reset/reload); `androscan/web/settings_routes.py` (`GET/PUT /api/settings/global`, `POST /api/settings/global/raw`, `POST /api/settings/global/reset`, per-app GET/PUT/reset, `POST /api/settings/reload`; validates raw YAML server-side; atomic write-back via tempfile + `os.replace`; returns `restart_required` per field). `androscan/config/loader.py` grew `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` (Config field → `(yaml_section, yaml_key, env_var)`), `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS`, `global_view_from_config`, `effective_sources` (returns `yaml`/`env`/`default` per field for source pills), `with_overrides`, `coerce_yaml_value`, `dump_to_yaml`, `save_raw_yaml`, `restore_defaults_yaml`, `validate_raw_yaml`, `read_raw_yaml`, `discover_config_path`. `app.py` refactored to keep the live `Config` on `app.state.config` (`_current_config`/`_set_config` helpers) so live-reloadable fields take effect without uvicorn restart; new routers read via callable providers. Frontend: `SettingsTab` + 4 sub-panels (`GlobalSettingsPanel` with form + raw-YAML editor + dirty state + reset; `AppSettingsPanel` with dropdown + override-or-inherit + force-with-warning reset; `StatusPanel` with cards + per-card `HealthDot` + "Refresh now"; `DiagnosticsPanel` with raw API JSON + `/api/settings/reload`). New `HealthDot` in the global header polls `/api/status/global` every 30 s and rolls up to a single green/yellow/red dot that deep-links to Settings on click. Two new API clients (`api/settings.ts`, `api/status.ts`) and ~300 lines of new CSS in `App.css` (`.source-badge`, `.env-lock`, `.restart-pill`, `.status-card`, `.health-dot.{green,yellow,red}`, `.settings-nav`, `.yaml-editor`). Tests: `tests/test_health_probes.py` (subprocess + urllib mocks; 20 cases), `tests/test_per_app_settings.py` (load/save/reset/merge/atomic), `tests/test_settings_routes.py` (integration). All Python tests green; `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` clean.
- notes: One bug discovered during testing — the integration test was monkeypatching `androscan.web.health_probes.probe_adb_version`, but `androscan.web.status_routes` imports `probe_adb_version` *by name*, so the patch never reached the consumer. Fix: monkeypatch the **consumer** module (`androscan.web.status_routes`). Documented inline in `test_settings_routes.py` and in DEC-021. The "force-with-warning" per-app reset semantics + the `restart_required` pill for non-live-reloadable fields were both explicitly user-confirmed during design.
- follow-up: (P2) Migrate hot-path handlers (`/api/llm/info`, chat, RAG, decompile) to read from `app.state.config` so live reload covers their fields. (P2) "Compare to global" diff view in the per-app panel. (P2) Persist raw-YAML draft to sessionStorage. (when Hook Lab starts) Add a "Hook Lab readiness" rollup probe.

### 2026-04 Phase 6 polish (UX step 3, partial): Lane-1 RAG indexer
- outcome: New `androscan/rag/` package (`chunking.py`, `embed.py`, `index.py`, `search.py`) implementing a per-app SQLite vector index of method-level chunks over the jadx decompiled sources. Brace-balanced chunker for `.java`/`.kt` (no tree-sitter). `EmbedProvider` protocol with `FastEmbedProvider` (default ONNX local), `OllamaEmbedProvider` (HTTP), and `HashProvider` (deterministic, test/no-deps fallback). Per-app store at `apps/<app_id>/.decompiled/<sha>/rag.sqlite` (WAL, packed `float32` blobs); brute-force cosine top-k (numpy when present, pure-Python fallback). Endpoints `GET /api/rag/{app_id}/status`, `POST /api/rag/{app_id}/rebuild`, `POST /api/rag/{app_id}/query` in `androscan/web/rag_routes.py`; jadx completion auto-schedules a daemon-thread RAG build via `schedule_rag_build_after_decompile`. New `llm`-tier skill `search_decompiled_sources` (query, top_k, package_prefix, file_substr) — advertised in prompt catalog, fail-open. Inspect-tab chat (`androscan/web/chat.py::_enrich_inspect_with_rag`) appends top-k chunks as `code` attachments before guardrails run; fail-soft when RAG is unavailable. `Config` extended with `rag_embed_provider` / `rag_embed_model` / `rag_top_k_default`; `global_config.yaml` adds `rag:` section; env overrides `ANDROSCAN_RAG_*`. New `pyproject` extra `[rag]` (fastembed, numpy). New tests: `tests/test_rag.py`, `tests/test_rag_integration.py`, plus three RAG endpoint tests in `tests/test_web.py` (197 tests total, all passing).
- notes: Click-to-code backend was already in place via `POST /api/inspect/map` + `androscan/web/inspect_map.py` (foreground activity + uiautomator dump + handler grep) and persistent decompile cache (`/api/decompile`, `/api/code/{tree,file}`); package-scoped logcat (with stable Linux-UID filtering) and mirror online/offline (`/api/device/status`) were also already implemented. The remaining backend gap was the `resolve_ui_element` LLM skill — see the next entry. Multimodal chat attachments (PDF/CSV/screenshot) are deferred to after Hook Lab. `sqlite-vec` is reserved as a future drop-in replacement for the brute-force scan.
- follow-up: Done in two follow-up entries below — `resolve_ui_element` LLM skill + `/api/inspect/map` fusion (backend), and the Inspect-tab frontend wiring entry. Next is UX step 4 (Hook Lab — Smali graph + Frida).

### 2026-04 Phase 6 polish (UX step 3, partial): `resolve_ui_element` LLM skill + `/api/inspect/map` fusion
- outcome: New `androscan/skills/resolve_ui_element.py` — `llm`-tier deterministic fuser that takes the element + foreground activity + raw handler candidates from `inspect_map.find_handlers` and produces a single `best` answer with reasoning, plus a ranked `alternatives` list. Score model: handler-kind base (`findViewById` 1.00 > `onClick_near` 0.80 > `compose_id` 0.60 > `reference` 0.20 > rag-cosine), additive bonuses for foreground-activity match (+0.50), activity-named files (+0.10), and an early-line decay bonus (≤+0.10 for line 1, → 0 at line 200). Optional Lane-1 RAG enrichment: synthesises a free-text query from `text` > `content_desc` > short resource id, fetches top-k chunks, and feeds them through the same scorer (so a clean `findViewById` in the foreground activity always beats an arbitrary semantic match). Pure-function `resolve()` is reused inline by `POST /api/inspect/map` (`androscan/web/app.py`) which now returns a `resolution` block (`{best, alternatives, rag_hits, reasoning}`) so the UI gets one ranked answer without an LLM round-trip. Skill is fail-soft on missing app dir / decompile cache / embed provider. Registered in `androscan/skills/__init__.py`. New tests: `tests/test_resolve_ui_element.py` (13 tests — pure helpers, deterministic scoring, foreground boost, early-line tie-break, RAG enrichment with `HashProvider`, fail-soft, registry path); `tests/test_web.py::test_api_inspect_map_returns_element_and_candidates` extended to assert the new `resolution` block. **210 tests total, all passing.**
- notes: This closes the *backend* of Phase 6 step 3. The frontend wiring landed in the next entry below.
- follow-up: Inspect-tab frontend wiring (done — see next entry); then UX step 4 (Hook Lab — Smali graph + Frida).

### 2026-04 Phase 6 polish (UX step 3, final): Inspect-tab frontend wiring for the fuser
- outcome: Surfaced the `resolution` block from `POST /api/inspect/map` end-to-end in the Inspect tab UI. Extended `androscan/web/frontend/src/api/inspect.ts` with `Resolution`, `ResolutionCandidate`, `ResolutionRagHit` types. New sub-components in `ElementMappingPanel.tsx`: `BestBanner` (regex/RAG `SourceBadge`, kind pill, score, reasons grid, snippet, "Open in Code browser"), `ResolutionAlternatives` (collapsible details with per-alt source badge + score + open button), `RagHitsList` (collapsible details with synthesised query and per-hit class.method label). `InspectTab.handleTap` now prefers `resolution.best` over `candidates[0]` when auto-opening the Code Browser, seeds `scrollTarget` + `highlightRange` (via `requestAnimationFrame` so re-clicks still fire), and propagates `best.method_name` so the method-emphasis label is correct. New CSS: `.source-badge` (deterministic = green, rag = warn), `.best-banner` (accent border + soft background, reasons grid), `.resolution-alts` / `.rag-hits` collapsibles. Keep this section concise.

---

## Task writing rules

Each task should be:
- specific
- scoped
- reviewable
- testable
- bounded in time and complexity

Avoid tasks like:
- “improve architecture”
- “make app better”
- “add security”
- “clean everything up”

Prefer tasks like:
- “add normalized finding model for first feature module”
- “implement adapter wrapper for tool X behind interface Y”
- “add negative tests for malformed artifact input in module Z”

---

## Phase and task list rules

**So other IDEs and agents know where to take over:**

1. **List sub-tasks explicitly.** For every phase or multi-step task, the implementation plan in this file MUST list sub-tasks explicitly (numbered or bulleted), not only in prose. Each sub-task should be verifiable on its own.

2. **Update status as soon as a sub-task is completed.** When a sub-task is finished (code done, tests pass, behaviour verified), update this file in the same work session:
   - Mark the sub-task done (e.g. add `[x] Done` or `— [x] Done` next to the heading, or update a status table).
   - Do not leave a phase "in progress" without reflecting which sub-tasks are already done.

3. **Keep a short status overview.** For the active phase, keep a compact status table or checklist (e.g. "Sub-task status") at the top of the implementation plan so the next agent can see at a glance what is done and what is next.

4. **External tool availability.** When adding or changing code that uses an external tool (apktool, jadx, etc.), the implementation must check tool availability (e.g. `shutil.which(cmd)`) and handle missing tools without crashing (return a clear result; do not raise raw subprocess/OS errors). See `docs/CONVENTIONS.md` §4 External tool availability.

---

## Default execution rule

Unless explicitly instructed otherwise:

- do one task at a time
- complete it properly
- add tests
- update docs
- then move to the next task
